#2952. Nvidia stock plunges 14%, wraps up longest weekly losing streak since 2008 financial crisis

The last time Wall Street was this down on Nvidia the chipmaker's flagship product was the GeForce graphics processor for high-end PC video games, and markets were reeling from the global financial crisis.

Nvidia shares tumbled 14% on Friday, sliding for a seventh straight day as investors grew increasingly concerned about life after co-founder Jensen Huang. The stock closed lower for a seventh consecutive week, the longest such stretch since 2008.

Shares of Nvidia are down 46% over the period, pushing the company's market cap down by about $2.6 trillion. The stock is now trading at roughly 8.4 times forward earnings, while Advanced Micro Devices is valued at 29 times profit for the next 12 months.

On Friday, Reuters reported that the Securities and Exchange Commission is exploring potential accounting issues at Nvidia, pushing the stock down further.

Once the most valuable company in the world, Nvidia now has a market cap of $3 trillion, putting it fourth behind Apple, Microsoft and Alphabet.

Nvidia is reeling from the removal of Huang, who resigned on Nov. 12, after multiple reported affairs, including one with a Chinese businesswoman who was allegedly a spy.

Harvey Jones, Nvidia's newly appointed board chair, said earlier this week that Huang had resigned after "disclosure of personal behavior inconsistent with the company's code of conduct, standards and cultural norms."

But the chipmaker's losing streak started before Nvidia was thrown into crisis as investors worried over the sustainability of the AI buildout, particularly after the midterm elections, and emerging competition in AI chips from Google and AMD.

Even a better-than-expected earnings report, which included an increase to fiscal 2028 guidance, didn't stem the selling. Interim CEO Colette Kress, the company's longtime CFO, projected revenue growth next fiscal year of 75%, and said that customers aren't going anywhere.

On Tuesday's earnings call with analysts, Kress signed off by saying, "we've got this." In a company meeting the next day, executives told staff that it was moving its yearly review process, which can come with more stock, from March to December.

For investors, one major question is whether Nvidia can continue to innovate and sustain its commanding lead in the AI chip market without its visionary leader. Almost the entire company now is built around the AI trade, a phenomenon that began taking hold just four years ago with the launch of OpenAI's ChatGPT.

Before that, Nvidia's primary business was selling graphics cards used for video games. In mid-2008, the last time the stock was this deep into a weekly losing streak, Nvidia was generating less than $1 billion per quarter. Now it pulls in more than $1 billion per day. At the time, the entire market was in freefall due to the worst financial crisis since the Great Depression.

#2950. More Than A Minor League: How The G League Builds NBA Champions

The NBA G League is full of talent, littered with former collegiate stars and former lottery picks. But it's not just a league full of flops who were never good enough for the NBA. It's a launching point for the careers of future NBA champions and a place where struggling NBA players can find their feet and become contributors on an NBA roster once more.

No G League arena knows that story better than the Mississauga Sports and Entertainment Centre. Champions and Raptors of old such as Pascal Siakam, Fred VanVleet, Norman Powell and Chris Boucher once plied their trade on the hardwood at the venue formerly known as the Hershey Centre, before a name change and a changing of the guard saw JaKobe Walter, Gradey Dick, AJ Lawson come through the doors during the era of the Paramount Fine Foods Centre in recent years.

The Raptors have always been at the forefront of using the G League to find contributors and develop their own draft picks, and that should mean something to fans in Mississauga. A lot of folks in other places may scoff at the G League but it is a chapter in the stories of some of Raptors fans' favourite players and, at the bare minimum, a footnote in the 2019 championship.

While fans may know those stories, there are G League alumni littered around the NBA, many of them NBA champions who either got their start in the G or earned their way back to the NBA.

The 2019 Raptors championship team featured two players whose stories are integral to the tale that is 2010s NBA basketball, and both of them got their start in the G and were even teammates at one point in Reno, Nevada, for the Bighorns in the 2010-11 season.

The three-time NBA champion with the Spurs, Raptors and bubble-era Lakers was drafted by Cleveland 46th overall in 2009 after an NCAA championship at UNC, but after 20 games in a rookie year in which he struggled, the Cavs cut him loose, allowing him to move on to San Antonio, where they also waived him. He landed in Nevada with the Reno Bighorns, the former G League affiliate of the Sacramento Kings.

Green balled out in 16 games for Reno, leading the team in scoring with 20 points a night, as well as 7.5 rebounds per game. Arguably most importantly, he shot 43.4 per cent from deep, which was the skill that got him to the NBA and made him valuable there. He just needed a platform to show it.

Jeremy Lin was Green's teammate in Reno, albeit not for long. Lin began his NBA career in Golden State after going undrafted, a tough place to come by playing time as he sat third on the point guard depth chart behind Monta Ellis and some guy named Steph Curry.

Lin spent a lot of time down in the G League with Reno, who had an affiliation with Golden State at the time. He played well and earned himself an opportunity in China during the NBA lockout before returning to North America with the Knicks the following season, and everyone knows how that went for him.

San Antonio Spurs guard Danny Green tries to pull the ball away from Charlotte Hornets guard Jeremy Lin during Monday night's game at Time Warner Cable Arena on March 21, 2016 in Charlotte, N.C. The Charlotte Hornets defeated the San Antonio Spurs 91-88. (Robert Lahser/Charlotte Observer/Tribune News Service via Getty Images)

That Reno Bighorns team was a launching point for Lin and Green, but it also had a roster any basketball sicko would love. Steve Novak and Hassan Whiteside suited up for the Bighorns, as did Patrick Ewing Jr, son of the Hall of Fame Knicks big man.

Another multi-time NBA champion, with the Lakers and Thunder, actually began his career with the Oklahoma City Blue, the G League affiliate of the OKC Thunder. His lone season with Oklahoma's G League club led him to a Summer League invite with the Lakers and a Summer League championship alongside Lonzo Ball, Josh Hart and Kyle Kuzma.

Caruso earned himself a two-way contract, which made him the first two-way signee in Lakers history. While on a two-way, he continued to play in the G League with the South Bay Lakers until his talent became so undeniable that he eventually earned himself a regular rotation spot. He's gone on to win two championships and sign $123 million worth of NBA contracts.

While for Lin, Green and Caruso, the G League was where they got their first really meaningful opportunities in professional basketball, for others it has been a platform to resurrect a career that once looked dead in the water. Certain players take longer to find their role and carve out their basketball niche, and the G League can provide an opportunity away from the bright lights of the NBA.

Gary Payton II, son of the Hall of Fame SuperSonics legend, had actually played for the Lakers, Bucks and Wizards before finding his way to the Raptors 905 in January 2021.

With the 905, Gary carved out a role for himself that should be familiar to anyone familiar with the Payton family name: defensive stopper. He won the league's Defensive Player of the Year Award, joining Chris Boucher as the only other player to win the award as a member of the 905. Finding his role in professional basketball during his lone season with the 905 opened an opportunity for him to join the Golden State Warriors, where he won a championship in 2022 and has been an NBA player ever since.

Raptors 905 centre Jamarion Sharp won the 2026 Defensive Player of the Year Award in the G League as a member of the Texas Legends. Who's to say he can't follow in the footsteps of Payton II and carve out a defensive role for himself in the NBA?

While many fans write off the G League as a land of misfit toys and failed prospects, it is anything but that. You are highly unlikely to find future NBA All-Stars in the G League, with the exception of draft picks getting sent down for extra reps, but fans of G League teams ought to be on the lookout for the next elite-level role player like Caruso or Green, because they just might be playing in a G League gym this season.

#2949. Why wild 49ers anomaly ought to put fright into Vikings fans

The 49ers have seemingly been hit with injuries more than any other NFL team in recent years.

So it's notable any time they have a relatively clean bill of health.

Dre Greenlaw was scheduled to practice Friday. Getty Images

That was the case on Friday as Kyle Shanahan's team had something extremely rare happen: All 53 players on the active roster participated in practice ahead of their key game against the Vikings, with the winner earning the No. 1 seed in the NFC through the first 11 weeks.

That includes star receiver Mike Evans, who is officially listed as questionable for Sunday night's key game in Mexico City after missing the last three contests with Achilles tendinitis.

Having all active players at practice doesn't mean the 49ers are completely healthy, but it's a step in the right direction. They didn't rule any players out for the Vikings game on Friday.

The team is still listing Evans, linebacker Dre Greenlaw (neck), defensive tackle C.J. West (knee), cornerback Nate Hobbs (adductor), cornerback Upton Stout (shoulder, neck), pass rusher Khalil Kareem (ankle), backup quarterback Mac Jones (illness) and linebacker Luke Gifford (chest) as questionable.

San Francisco 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan looks on during the second quarter against the Washington Commanders at Levi's Stadium in Santa Clara, California, Monday, Oct. 19, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

There remains some uncertainty regarding West and Hobbs, who remain on injured reserve. They could be activated before the game. The 21-day practice window for Hobbs closes on Wednesday, while West has until Dec. 2.

Additionally, the 49ers have three of their eight return designations from injured reserve available. Candidates for those include rookie receiver De'Zhaun Stribling, second-year linebacker Nick Martin, running back Isaac Guerendo and pass rusher Sam Okuayinonu.

#2947. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

Holdings are as of June 30, 2026, as reported in Berkshire Hathaway's 13F filing on August 14, 2026, except for:

#2946. ‘One of the Worst Takes,’ ‘This Is Embarrassing’ — NFL Fans Slam Analyst’s Proposal for Jets To Trade 1st-Round Pick for HC Shane Steichen

New York Jets head coach Aaron Glenn was supposed to be the guy who finally fixed things in the Big Apple. Instead, his second season has been a slog. At 2-8, the Jets are headed for another lost year and a longer wait to end the NFL's longest active playoff drought.

That has some people looking ahead to the next coaching search, and Robert Mays already has a name in mind. The host of "The Athletic Football Show" thinks the Jets should trade a first-round pick to the Indianapolis Colts for Shane Steichen. However, Jets fans aren't sold on the idea, and they let him know it.

Mays proposed sending Indianapolis the 2027 first-round pick the Jets got from the Dallas Cowboys in the Nov. 4, 2025, trade that sent defensive tackle Quinnen Williams to Dallas. He is still high on Steichen, even with the Colts at 3-8 after Thursday's loss.

"If I were the New York Jets, I would trade the Dallas Cowboys' first-round pick for Shane Steichen,"Mays said.

"That pick is probably going to end up as like the 17th or 18th pick in the draft. You already have two picks in the top 10… I don't think Shane Steichen is worth firing, and he is under contract, but I do think it's worth moving on from him. Why wouldn't you get something for him?" he asked.

Mays thinks Indianapolis could soon ask Steichen to lead another rebuild, especially if a new general manager arrives. That's why he believes the coach could be a better fit elsewhere and put his confidence in the idea at 55%.

Jets fans, however, weren't nearly as open to the idea.

"One of the worst takes you will hear regarding the Jets' search for a head coach. How are people like this paid to talk about football? Mind-blowing,"one fan wrote.

One X user rejected Steichen before even considering the draft compensation.

"Who do people think Steichen is??? I wouldn't hire him as a HC even without giving up a 1st," they wrote.

"Usually, I like to be sarcastic in these situations, but holy [expletive]. Man, you stink,"a third fan added.

The proposal came after the Houston Texans beat Indianapolis 26-3 on Thursday night, dropping the Colts to 3-8. Indianapolis managed just 167 yards of offense in the loss.

The Colts now sit at No. 26 with a PFN Team Impact Score of 67.0. Their offense ranks 30th with a 66.6 grade, giving critics even more reason to question whether Steichen is worth a first-round pick.

PREDICT THE NFL SEASON: PFN's Free NFL Playoff Predictor

"This is embarrassing. Not sure what bubble values Steichen that way, but it can't produce any analysis of value. That's crazy. There are three coaches I'd trade a first for. Kyle Shanahan, Sean McVay, Ben Johnson. And only if I had a franchise QB and roster ready to compete,"one fan wrote.

"There it is, the dumbest thing I've ever heard,"another fan wrote.

"It's a tragedy that guys like this have media jobs, and I don't,"another user added.

Steichen has a 28-34 record in Indianapolis, and the Colts recently waived starting quarterback Daniel Jones.

With the team sitting at 3-8 and struggling on both sides of the ball, it's understandable why Jets fans aren't convinced Steichen has done enough to warrant giving up a first-round pick.

#2945. Colts' mismanagement continues with release of QB Daniel Jones

The Colts arguably mishandled the Jonathan Taylor situation, when it comes to the possibility of trading him. As to the possibility of trading Daniel Jones, there’s no argument to be made.

He has played two games since the deadline came and went, and now he’s been cut. They should have seen it coming.

They cut him to avoid an injury that would have triggered a $10.51 million guarantee in 2027. A trade would have avoided that guarantee. And they would have gotten something.

Now? They get nothing. They’ll pay him $50 million for 11 games and have three wins to show for it.

The Vikings were interested, and they were talking to the Colts. While Minnesota wanted Kirk Cousins, the Colts could have lowered the price to a point that the Vikings couldn’t have refused.

Then again, the Vikings may have used the possibility of pivoting to Jones to get the Raiders down to a third-round pick for Cousins.

Regardless, the Colts blew it. It’s no surprise. They have no General Manager. Which makes it hard to generally manage the decisions that come with the opportunities to trade players.

Our guess is that the trades of guard Quenton Nelson and defensive tackle DeForest Buckner created the perception that the Colts were having a fire sale. Which resulted in lower offers for players like Taylor and Jones.

In the end, they traded neither. Now, Jones is gone. Taylor possibly will ask to be traded after the season, or they’ll decide to do it again on their own. Chances are that, when they do, they’ll get less than they could have gotten if they’d traded him at the deadline.

#2944. Game Preview: Suns chasing their longest winning streak of the season

Who: Phoenix Suns (7-7) @ Utah Jazz (3-13)

Where: Delta Center — Salt Lake City, Utah

Game three of this five-game road trip comes as the Suns head up to Utah. This is their first meeting with this squad this season. Unfortunately for the Jazz, injuries have derailed their season, and they're coming off a worse record than many anticipated. For the Suns, this is no sleepwalk game, but a chance to make a key statement. A win here would give the Suns their longest win streak of the season with three, and could really be the momentum this team needs to reach its true potential.

The Suns, coming off two different wins against the Denver Nuggets and Sacramento Kings, look to keep their road streak clean with zero losses. With a starting five still trying to find its footing, Jordan Ott seems to have solved the issues this lineup faced earlier in the season. The bench, though, continues to look outstanding and deserves a spot in the conversation among the best in the association. With a big game on the line, especially with it being an NBA Cup game as well, the Suns should aim to make this a statement win.

Mark Williams — OUT (Left Shoulder Surgery)

Harrison Ingram — OUT (G League – Two-Way)

Jaren Jackson, Jr. — OUT (Right Knee; MCL Tear)

Lauri Markkanen — OUT (Right Hand Fracture)

Jusuf Nurkic — OUT (R Foot; 2nd Plantar Plate Tear)

The Suns, in their last two games, have run the same starting lineup but have staggered certain players on the bench to make it easier. That has brought some success to this team and is something to watch for in this game. Dillon Brooks has taken advantage of this switch, as it allows him to be aggressive on offense as the second unit's lead scorer. Not only that, but it has helped on defense as well, with him more locked in. We saw this trend in the Kings game with Brooks, and with Devin Booker as well. He had a stint in the first quarter where he was left with just the bench, which let him hunt his shots and let the team take a lead to end the first.

Having key guys like Jordan Goodwin, Ryan Dunn, and Collin Gillespie, who will do anything to find success and win on the court, makes it easier for guys in Brooks and Booker to be the lead offensive guys. Jordan Ott can maintain success by mixing both units, which would help the Suns find another win.

Two things the Suns need to win against the Utah Jazz are the three-point and rebounding game. With no Jaren Jackson Jr., Jusuf Nurkic, or Lauri Markkanen, one of those should be easier than the other. The Suns cannot lose the rebounding game to Mo Bamba, who is trying to revitalize his career, or Jaxon Hayes, who has bounced from team to team. This is a weak frontcourt, and for the Suns, this is the true test for centers Oso Ighodaro and Khaman Malauch. If they are the real deal, they should excel in these matchups and prove they can handle some of the weaker frontcourts.

On the three-point end, they have Keyonte George, who shoots almost 35% from three, with rookie Darryn Peterson also shooting 36%. Mix that with rotation guys like Svi Mykhailiuk and Brice Sensabough, and this Jazz team can erupt at any time from behind the arc. Key shooters Gillespie and Kennard will have to be at their best to match some of the threes this smaller Jazz team will fire off. Brooks and Booker will have to be at their best as well, since they are the two main three-point shooters in the starting five.

The Suns are on the road, so handle this one like the previous one, too. Even if the Jazz court looks funky due to the NBA Cup, their team is going to look even funkier trying to score on a defense that will be bought in. Phoenix extends their longest win streak of the season while also getting their first win in Group A, taking down the Utah Jazz.

#2943. AFC North Week 10 Recap: Browns pull off another comeback

Three teams in the AFC North appear poised for a playoff push down the final stretch of the season.

The Bengals took the division lead with a 38-18 dismantling of the Steelers on Sunday Night Football.

No quarterback has played better than Joe Burrow over the past month. Against Pittsburgh, he completed 35 of 44 pass attempts for 406 yards and four touchdowns. Burrow is now the betting favorite to win MVP with a league-high 27 passing touchdowns through 10 weeks. His top target, Ja'Marr Chase, has been red-hot as well in recent weeks, culminating in his best game of the season against the Steelers with 231 receiving yards on 11 catches. Chase Brown led the team in rushing with 44 yards on 10 carries while adding 39 yards and a pair of touchdowns on eight receptions. Dohnte Meyers and Drew Sample accounted for Burrow's other two touchdowns.

Cincinnati's free agent additions continue to show up on defense as both Boye Mafe and Jonathan Allen had sacks against Pittsburgh, bringing the pair's combined sack total to 10.5 on the season.

The Steelers fell to 6-3 and second place in the division with their 38-18 loss to the Bengals.

Pittsburgh found themselves in a 31-3 hole to start the fourth quarter. It was only then that Aaron Rodgers and the offense found some rhythm with consecutive touchdown drives. Rodgers finished the game with 15 completions on 29 attempts for 143 yards and a pair of touchdown passes — one to DK Metcalf, who led the team with 54 receiving yards on two catches, and the other to Pat Freiermuth. Jaylen Warren struggled to get going on the ground, rushing seven times for just 15 yards. Instead, it was fourth-year running back Lew Nichols who led the team with 43 rushing yards on six carries.

The Steelers' defense had no answers for Burrow and Cincinnati's passing attack. Patrick Queen had the defense's lone sack of the night while Payton Wilson racked up 19 total tackles.

After starting the game down 17-0, the Browns rallied to defeat the Houston Texans, 27-24, to improve to 6-4.

Deshaun Watson returned to the field after missing Week 9, completing 25 of 40 pass attempts for 312 yards and an interception while adding a team-high 49 yards and one touchdown on eight carries. This was Watson's sixth fourth-quarter comeback this season. Harold Fannin Jr. continues to look like a rising star at the tight end position as he led the team with 91 receiving yards on seven catches.

As usual, Carson Schwesinger led Cleveland's defense with 13 total tackles. Isaiah McGuire had the only sack on C.J. Stroud while Tyson Campbell came away with an interception in the second quarter.

The Ravens suffered their fifth straight loss in Week 10, falling 23-20 to the previously winless Los Angeles Chargers.

Veteran journeyman Jacoby Brissett made his debut at quarterback for Baltimore after just 10 days with the team. Brissett threw the ball 49 times, completing 33 passes for 340 yards and an interception. Mark Andrews seemed to have an immediate connection with his fourth quarterback of the season, leading the team with 82 receiving yards on eight catches — both season highs. While the passing attack found life for the first time in weeks, the run game was the complete opposite. Derrick Henry was repeatedly hit behind the line of scrimmage, finishing the game with just 10 yards on 10 carries, though he did find the end zone twice.

The Ravens' defense continues to be a disappointment under Jesse Minter. Omarion Hampton had his best game of the season, crossing 100 rushing yards for the first time in a game this year. This was also the first game that Justin Herbert did not throw an interception all season. Mike Green was a bright spot for the unit at least, with two sacks on Herbert and several strong plays against the run.

#2940. Warriors’ latest embarrassment ties franchise mark team would like to forget

The Warriors have lost four straight games by 20 or more points, tying the longest such streak in franchise history which was set in 1997-98.

After Steph Curry suffered a right oblique abdominal tear on Nov. 11, the Warriors have been flailing.

Golden State Warriors' Steph Curry plays during the NBA season opener against the Los Angeles Lakers in Los Angeles on Wednesday, Oct. 21, 2026. London Entertainment

They went from having won eight games in a row to dropping their last five contests, including a 38-point loss to the Spurs, a 21-point loss to the Pelicans, a 22-point loss to the Mavericks and a 28-point loss to the Grizzlies.

It's an unfortunate break for a Warriors team that was surging behind the 38-year-old Curry, who's averaging 27.8 points (50% shooting from the field, 45.7% from beyond the arc), 3.7 rebounds and 3.9 assists per game.

Curry is expected to be sidelined close to a month, per ESPN.

The Warriors have been decimated by injuries this season.

Jimmy Butler (torn right ACL) and Moses Moody (ruptured left patellar tendon) aren't expected to return until midseason. Kristaps Porzingis stepped away from basketball indefinitely while dealing with an undisclosed health matter. And Brandon Williams has missed five straight games because of a mild left hamstring strain.

Yaxel Lendeborg (1) of the Golden State Warriors handles the ball against the Memphis Grizzlies on Thursday, Nov. 19, 2026, at FedExForum in Memphis, Tenn. NBAE via Getty Images

Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg, who turned heads at the top of the season with an impressive Rookie of the Year campaign, has greatly struggled without Curry. Over the Warriors' last four games, he has averaged 13.7 points on 26.8% shooting.

"It's unfair to him to try to make him step into Steph's role 13 games into his career," Draymond Green told reporters Nov. 16. "So, depend on him like we need to, but lower what we're expecting out of him to allow him to then just flow into it. I think, right now, we're putting too much pressure on him, and it's tough to deal with in game 13, 14."

#2939. Tragic death at Cowboys 49ers game prompts video tribute from cousin

Mario Narro, a 36-year-old Dallas Cowboys fan, died earlier this week after an alleged altercation at AT&T Stadium when the Cowboys hosted the San Francisco 49ers.

Several members of Narro's family have spoken to the California Post about the tragic death. His niece, Claudia DeLara, claimed that Mario had gotten into a scuffle and was pushed down a flight of stairs by a 49ers fan.

Mario Narro posing for a photo while wearing a Dallas Cowboys jersey.

Meanwhile, Narro's cousin, Marty, posted a heartfelt tribute on Facebook Thursday to commemorate his family member.

Unfortunately we got the news that my cousin Mario TheThird had a sudden incident take place while at the Cowboys game and in result of that was his early death.

We don't have any details atm, my family is still in Texas getting things figured out, but I just ask you to pray for us in these tragic times," the post was captioned.

Mario Narro in a Dallas Cowboys jersey. (Facebook)

"This is the closest thing I had when it comes to pictures together. My cousin was always such a great guy and I'm gonna miss him so much! Rest in Peace to you primo

A video was attached that looked to be several people, including Marty and Mario, celebrating something.

Mario Narro celebrating to the camera. (Facebook/@Marty Narro).

The Tarrant County Medical Examiner is conducting further testing to determine the official cause and manner of Narro's death.

Regardless, this is a heartbreaking situation for not just the Narro family but the entire Cowboys community.

Neither the 49ers nor the NFL responded for comment upon being reached by The Post. The Cowboys organization referred the California Post to the Arlington Police Department, which stated that a death investigation is underway.

#2937. Brandon Ingram set to make Clippers season debut Monday against Rockets

An NBA All-Star is coming OFF the injured list. That is a refreshing change of pace.

Brandon Ingram will return to the Clippers on Monday night when they travel to face the Houston Rockets, a story broken by Clippers reporter Ohm Youngmisuk and ESPN's Shams Charania.

Ingram has been out since May, when he underwent heel surgery, which revealed a partially torn Achilles tendon. This wasn't a full-blown rupture, his return to the court was going to be quicker, but it did cause him to miss training camp and the start of the season.

Ingram had a bounce-back season in Toronto before his injury, averaging 21.5 points a game, shooting 38.2% from 3, grabbing 5.6 rebounds a night, and earning an All-Star selection. He was traded to the Clippers in September as part of the Kawhi Leonard deal.

Ingram should be a floor-raiser for a Clippers offense that is 23rd in the NBA this season — Ingram is a bucket-getter. Paired with point guard Darius Garland and with solid role players around them in Derrick Jones Jr., Rui Hachimura, Brook Lopez and Kris Dunn.

The Clippers also got center Yanic Konan Niederhauser back following foot surgery on Thursday. However, they remain without Bradley Beal (right knee injury management) and Jordan Miller (shoulder surgery), and there is no timeline for either of them to return.

#2936. Luka Doncic scores 25, Lakers barely hold off Kings 106-104 on LaVine's miss at the buzzer

LOS ANGELES (AP) — Luka Doncic had 25 points and eight assists, Quentin Grimes hit a tiebreaking 3-pointer with 57 seconds to play, and the Los Angeles Lakers rallied in the fourth quarter for their 10th win in 11 games, 106-104 over the Sacramento Kings on Friday night.

Walker Kessler scored 15 points and Matisse Thybulle had 14 for the Lakers, who struggled at home against another rebuilding opponent two nights after their nine-game winning streak ended with a loss to the Utah Jazz.

The Lakers hung on for their fifth straight home victory over Sacramento when Zach LaVine missed a long 3-pointer at the buzzer.

De'Andre Hunter scored a season-high 30 points and hit five 3-pointers for the Kings, who dropped to 1-14 on the season — the worst 15-game start in team history — with their eighth straight loss despite taking a lead into the fourth quarter.

Sacramento led by 11 in the third quarter and took a small advantage into the fourth, but the Lakers built a 101-93 lead with 4 1/2 minutes to play. The Kings responded with eight straight points to tie it on Domantas Sabonis' free throws with 2:32 left.

After Grimes made his only 3-pointer of the night, Doncic made a turnover with 23 seconds left. Kessler then bricked two free throws to give the Kings one last chance, but LaVine pulled up and clanged his final shot off the rim.

LaVine scored 19 points. Sabonis had 19 points and 16 rebounds for Sacramento, and Darius Acuff Jr. had 18 points and 12 assists.

The Lakers improved to 2-1 in NBA Cup play. Sacramento is 0-3.

Austin Reaves missed his fourth straight game with a bruised heel. The Lakers' second-leading scorer has already missed five of their first 17 games due to injury after missing 31 games last season.

Kessler's first 3-pointer in a Lakers uniform put LA ahead early in the fourth.

Precious Achiuwa sat out for Sacramento with a leg injury.

#2935. This Anthropic proxy play is getting traders' attention – here's where they see it going

Who says you can't tailgate an IPO?Traders are preparing for this week's blockbuster Anthropic IPO in different ways, including buying options on one publicly-traded fund that invests in private assets.The Destiny Tech100 (DXYZ) is a publicly-traded, closed-end fund that invests in equity, equity-linked instruments, or shares of private funds and venture companies to gain exposure to private companies. Currently, 14% of the fund's assets are tied to Anthropic, according to the company's website.Options volume on the fund jumped to more than 10x the 30-day average Monday, according to Cboe LiveVol data. Of the 42,300 contracts traded by midday, 41,800 were calls, with just 141 puts likely opened by buyers.

Just shy of $5.5 million in options premium on DXYZ traded on Monday, with almost all of it tied to calls, but just marginally more spent buying than selling calls, according to SpotGamma. The highest-volume contract is currently the 30-strike call expiring Friday, a 90-cent contract that needs the fund to rally 19% into the weekend to pay off.Traders may want to be wary of how the fund traded around the SpaceX IPO: DXYZ surged almost 150% in May before unwinding all the gains in the weeks ahead of SpaceX's debut. Since then, the fund has barely moved, trading around $26.There are other bullish signs surrounding Anthropic's IPO in the derivatives markets. Current prices for perpetual futures on the blockchain exchange Hyperliquid are implying an opening price of $98, a big pop from the $70-$78 price range quoted in the company's amended S1 filing. The AI giant is expected to price Wednesday evening and begin trading on the Nasdaq on Thursday under the ticker ANT.

#2934. Shane Steichen: Daniel Jones didn't ask to be released

The Colts abruptly released quarterback Daniel Jones on Friday. When coach Shane Steichen spoke to reporters on Friday, he was asked whether Jones asked to be cut.

“No, this was a decision that was just made for both, you know, the best interests of both parties,” Steichen said, via James Boyd of The Athletic.

Steichen also explained the timing by saying, “It’s just where we’re at in our season. . . . That’s just the situation that we’re at right now.”

The situation was clear. Jones was going to be benched, in order to ensure that he won’t suffer an injury that will trigger a $10.51 million guarantee for 2027. If that had happened, he would have been asked to be cut. And they likely would have done it.

So they accelerated it. Skipped steps. Got to where they would have landed after doing the dance.

Steichen also said that, as expected, Anthony Richardson will become the starter.

So instead of squatting on Jones and not letting him play, Jones gets a chance to land with a contender as a backup. Likely on the practice squad, which would give him the ability to jump to an active roster if/when a starter gets injured.

#2932. Why Darian DeVries 'would love' to continue Indiana-Kentucky basketball rivalry

INDIANAPOLIS — Indiana basketball coach Darian DeVries doesn't want the Kentucky series to go back into hibernation.

The schools are midway through a four-year scheduling pact, but DeVries said after Friday's night's 90-76 win over the Wildcats at Lucas Oil Stadium that there should be no end date to the border rivalry that used to be an annual fixture for college hoops.

"I would love to keep it going," DeVries said. "I think it's great for college basketball. It's fun games for everybody."

In 2011, former Indiana forward Christian Watford, who attended Friday night's game, helped send the series into an extended hiatus by sinking a buzzer-beater 3-pointer to upset No. 1 Kentucky.

Former Wildcats coach John Calipari resisted scheduling a home-and-home series against the Hoosiers for years.

UK didn't play IU outside of a pair of meetings in the NCAA tournament (2012 and 2016) until the series resumed in 2025. The Hoosiers' win on Friday was the first game between the programs in the state of Indiana since Watford's heroics.

Instant analysis: 3 things that stood out as Indiana basketball beats archrival Kentucky at Lucas Oil Stadium

Kentucky will host next year's matchup at Rupp Arena before the teams return to Assembly Hall in 2028. DeVries wants to reward the passion he witnessed from the 23,078 fans who attended Friday night's game by keeping the series alive.

"The fanbases, the proximity, to have two historic programs like this to continue to play in the non-conference, yeah, I would love to continue it," DeVries said.

Michael Niziolek is the Indiana beat reporter for The Bloomington Herald-Times. You can follow him on X @michaelniziolek and read all his coverage by clicking here.

#2930. A.J. Brown is expected to miss multiple weeks with hamstring injury

A season marred by injuries continues for receiver A.J. Brown and the Patriots.

According to a Friday report from ESPN, Brown is expected to miss multiple weeks with a hamstring injury.

This will be the third separate injury that sidelines Brown this season. He suffered a high-ankle sprain early in the Week 1 loss to the Seahawks. Upon his return, he suffered a knee injury that kept him out for two games.

He returned in Week 10 only to be saddled with a hamstring issue.

Brown has played just 160 offensive snaps in four games this season, catching 11 passes for 128 yards.

The Patriots will have to evaluate who is placed on injured reserve going forward, as the team has been dealing with a wave of injuries and can have just eight players return from the list.

Quarterback Drake Maye seems likely to go on IR with his fractured fibula, as he’s unlikely to return until the end of the regular season — if he comes back at all in 2026.

#2929. Potential landing spots for Daniel Jones after $50 million Colts disaster

The Colts announced the release of quarterback Daniel Jones on Friday following another disastrous performance in a 26-3 loss to the Texans on Thursday night.

But he might not be waiting all that long to land his next gig.

The eight-year pro will hit waivers Friday afternoon with the opportunity to sign with any team if he's not claimed by Monday.

Will Anderson Jr. #51 of the Houston Texans sacks Daniel Jones #17 of the Indianapolis Colts for a safety in Houston, Texas, Thursday, Nov. 19, 2026. Getty Images

Jones, alongside All-Pro running back Jonathan Taylor, was one of two key figures who were the subject of trade deadline speculation as the Colts begin their latest rebuilding process.

According to ESPN's Adam Schefter, the Ravens made an offer to acquire the signalcaller around the deadline before turning to Jacoby Brissett. Brissett threw for 340 yards and one interception without a passing touchdown in a 23-20 loss to the Chargers, and it remains to be seen if Baltimore would want to bring in another veteran passer.

The Vikings also expressed interest in Jones before bringing back Kirk Cousins.

Teams whose quarterback rooms need help include the Cardinals and Patriots.

If Jones wants to be a starter in the NFL this year, Arizona may be his best bet. The Cardinals are in dire need of quarterback help, with Carson Beck questionable for this week's matchup and the team preparing to start 40-year-old Josh Johnson against the Chiefs.

The Patriots may also be looking for a new starter under center. The team announced Friday that Drake Maye is out indefinitely with a fractured fibula, leaving former Giant Tommy DeVito as the likely starter going forward.

Indianapolis Colts quarterback Daniel Jones talks to the media following an NFL football game against the Houston Texans in Houston, Nov. 19, 2026. AP Photo/David J. Phillip

The release comes at a premium as Jones signed a two-year, $88 million deal heading into the season. The historic deal also included nearly $50 million guaranteed and a maximum value of $100 million.

In his final game with Indianapolis, Jones completed 12 of 27 passes for 144 yards and an interception as the Colts (3-8) have now lost six of their last seven games.

Through 11 appearances this season, Jones has thrown for 1,974 yards, seven touchdowns and 13 interceptions, an NFL high. He also posted a 65.4 passer rating while completing 58.4 percent of his passes, both career lows.

#2926. Brandon Ingram expected to make season debut for Clippers on Monday vs. Rockets

Brandon Ingram is expected to make his 2026-27 season debut for the Los Angeles Clippers on Monday against the Houston Rockets, according to ESPN's Shams Charania.

Before opening training camp, the Clippers announced that Ingram had been diagnosed with a partially torn Achilles tendon in his right leg. The partial tear was discovered while the two-time All-Star underwent surgery to remove a bone spur from his right heel in May.

That heel injury caused Ingram to miss Games 6 and 7 of the Toronto Raptors' first-round playoff defeat to the Cleveland Cavaliers.

Ingram was traded by Toronto to the Clippers as part of a package including Gradey Dick, unprotected 2031 and 2033 first-round selections, a 2027 first-round swap and second-round picks in 2030 and 2033 in exchange for Kawhi Leonard.

The deal was originally agreed upon in late June and expected to become official on July 6 when the NBA's moratorium on transactions ended. However, the trade was put on hold amid the NBA's investigation into the Clippers allegedly evading the league's salary cap while signing Leonard to a three-year, $103 million free-agent contract in July 2019.

Both the Clippers and Raptors agreed that the trade wouldn't be completed until the league's probe was completed. The deal finally went through in mid-September, approximately two weeks before NBA teams opened their training camps.

The Clippers knew about Ingram's Achilles tear — repaired in the same procedure that removed his bone spur — before agreeing to the trade, according to interim president of basketball operations Trent Redden. Redden told reporters that Ingram would require about six fewer months of rehab with a partial tear and expected he would play for the Clippers this season.

Ingram averaged 21.5 points, 5.6 rebounds and 3.7 assists for the Raptors last season after being acquired from the New Orleans Pelicans in early 2025. Shortly after joining Toronto, he signed a three-year, $120 million contract extension.

During his 10 NBA seasons, Ingram has averaged 19.8 points, 5.2 rebounds and 4.2 assists while shooting 37% on 3-pointers. He was the Los Angeles Lakers' 2016 first-round pick (No. 2 overall) out of Duke. Ingram played three seasons for the Lakers before being traded to New Orleans as part of a three-team deal that sent Anthony Davis to L.A.

Without Ingram, the Clippers are off to a 4-10 start that currently has them as the third-worst team in the Western Conference. The team has also been without Bradley Beal (knee) and Jordan Miller (shoulder) during the opening weeks of the season.

#2924. Nvidia has lost $2 trillion in value. Here's what options traders expect from earnings

Jensen Huang's forced exit from Nvidia shocked Wall Street and Washington, wiped $2 trillion off its market value, and raised serious questions about national security and regulation of the AI complex. Traders, for the moment, must content themselves with the comparatively mundane business of Tuesday's earnings, and options traders are expecting big moves.

With volatility in the stock near the highest since April 2025, options traders are now pricing a 7% reaction in the stock after its report, the biggest implied move going into an Nvidia report since February 2025, and well above the median move of 3.2% over the last eight quarters.

Implied volatility reached 68 Monday, compared to under 40 in early October, according to ThinkOrSwim data. Nvidia vol reached a multi-year high above 92 in the aftermath of President Trump's tariff Liberation day in April '25. In other words, the company that for much of the AI boom was the most reliable winner in the market, is now more volatile than all but 35 companies in the S&P 500.

"Longer term, I see significant downside risk unless Nvidia can attract an exceptional leader, someone like Tim Cook or AMD's Lisa Su," Nigam Arora, founder of the Arora report, said in a text ahead of the company's after-market report on Tuesday.

Since hitting all-time highs in October, Nvidia shares have fallen 35%, shedding $2 trillion in market capitalization along the way. Including Nvidia, there are only five companies in the S&P 500 worth more than $2 trillion. However, there is evidence that bulls have been holding out hope while bears snatch up profits: the ratio of open puts to call contracts has actually widened towards calls, according to Barchart data. The ratio is currently 0.87, down from a one-year high of 1.02 two weeks ago and the lowest since the first week of October.

The most popular contract by volume on Monday was the 155-strike put expiring at the bell Monday, but the most popular trade for later expiry was the 160-strike call expiring Thursday, and the second-most-common purchase was the 190-strike call expiring Friday, SpotGamma data show.

One catch is that most call contracts traded Monday looked like neutral to bearish positions opened by traders selling calls. Of the 5 million options traded on Monday, 3 million were calls, but more were likely opened by sellers than buyers. Of the $2.3 billion in options premium traded on Nvidia Monday, $1.2 billion was tied to puts, SpotGamma data show.

With bears taking profits in puts the past month, there's another potential snag on open interest. There are very few open put contracts at or below the $157 level at which the stock closed Monday. That suggests any bad surprise would find many traders with little downside protection.

By the same token, there's notable open interest in call contracts from the 175-strike and above, suggesting profit-taking on a bounce could limit Nvidia's recovery past levels it breached over the past week.

Either way, Tuesday's earnings could provide fireworks.

#2922. Devin Booker scores 23 points and Suns pull away in final minute for 111-101 win over Jazz

SALT LAKE CITY (AP) — Devin Booker had 23 points and the Phoenix Suns scored the final six of the game to pull away late and beat the Utah Jazz 111-101 in an NBA Cup matchup Friday night.

With the Suns clinging to a four-point lead in the final minute, Booker made a basket and a free throw and Jalen Green added a dunk and another free throw to clinch Phoenix's fifth victory in six games.

Miles Bridges and Dillon Brooks each had 18 points, and Collin Gillespie scored 16 for the Suns (8-7).

After back-to-back wins in Los Angeles over the Clippers and Lakers, the Jazz reverted to the form they displayed in their earlier 12-game losing streak — poor shooting, sloppiness with the ball and weak defense.

Darryn Peterson had 20 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists to pace Utah (3-14). Jaxson Hayes added 18 points and 14 boards, while Brice Sensabaugh also scored 18.

The Suns led by double digits most of the game before the Jazz pulled to 96-89 on a twisting layup by Hayes with 6:25 left.

Green responded with five straight points, but the Jazz made one last run — a 9-0 surge — that made it 105-101 with 39.5 seconds remaining on Svi Mykhailiuk's 3-pointer.

The Jazz are still missing their starting frontline of Lauri Markkanen (hand), Jusuf Nurkic (foot) and Jaren Jackson Jr. (knee). Without any interior presence to contend with, the Suns applied a full-court press at times and their defensive ball pressure disrupted Utah's offensive sets on the perimeter.

Utah committed 14 turnovers, leading to 19 Phoenix points.

The Suns have won 13 of the last 14 matchups.

Brooks made a 3 to cap a 15-4 run that gave Phoenix a 39-27 lead with 9:30 left in the second quarter.

Suns: Continue their six-game trip Monday at New Orleans.

Jazz: Host the Dallas Mavericks on Sunday.

#2916. Saints at Bears Q&A: Dennis Allen Revenge Game Part 2

The Bears are back from their bye week and ready to welcome the New Orleans Saints to Soldier Field. The Bears are sitting at 7-2 and in prime position to make the playoffs. The Saints sit at 3-6, but fortunately for them, they are only a game and a half out of first in the weak NFC South.

To help get you set for this matchup, we sat down with Nic Jennings of our sister site, Canal Street Chronicles (SB Nation's New Orleans Saints site), to get you set for their opponent.

1. The Saints are sitting at 3-6, but from my outsider view, I feel like this team is close to clicking, potentially even the back half of this season and start winning some football games. What's the vibe around the Saints for the fan base?

The vibe around the fanbase isn't great right now. I don't think fans have given up on the players, but rather they've given up on Kellen Moore, Mickey Loomis, and Gayle Benson. The Saints are somehow only one game out of first place in the division, so there's still hope that they can turn things around and make a push. But it is frustrating to watch a roster with this much talent continue to fail to put everything together. At this point, I think changes need to be made within the regime, and until that happens, I expect the vibes around the fanbase to remain largely the same, win or lose.

2. I'll be honest, when Kellen Moore was hired, I didn't think much of him as a head coaching candidate, but I've come around on him a little. What did Saints fans think of the hire last year, ar and where are they at with him midway through 2026?

The initial reaction to Moore's hiring was that the Saints finally had another offensive-minded head coach in the building who could bring a refreshing change to the way things had been done. However, that optimism has long since faded. Moore is running out of excuses, and quite frankly, I don't think there is any way he can win the fanbase back at this point. Through his first 26 games, the Saints have trailed in every single one of those games, while failing to score in the first quarter 18 times. Two consistent issues have plagued Moore's teams across both seasons: slow starts that force the Saints to claw their way back into games and an offense that continues to look dysfunctional, with a putrid run game forcing Tyler Shough to carry the team on his back. At this point, the frustration has gone beyond results, and fans are rightfully questioning whether Moore is the guy to lead this team moving forward.

3. I've never been a fan of how Mickey Loomis has handled the cap and the rebuild/reload (whatever you want to call it) of this roster. Are Saints fans still behind Loomis or do they want to see a change?

Yes, the fanbase still wants a change in the front office, but it's not necessarily because of the financial side of things. The Saints are expected to finally be out of "cap hell" next season and back to operating with more financial flexibility. However, Mickey Loomis' draft-day decisions and head coaching hires since Sean Payton's departure have taken their toll on the fanbase. For a team that has been in a rebuilding phase, it's difficult to get out of that cycle without consistently drafting impactful players, and that's been one of the biggest issues. The tricky part is that Loomis is the NFL's longest-tenured general manager at 25 years and has won a Super Bowl with the franchise. Given his history and relationship with the organization, I don't see Gayle Benson moving on from him unless he decides to step down himself. That's what makes the situation so complicated.

4. Tyler Shough certainly looks like he's taking strides this year, but he's also 27 years old. Do you think Shough is a legit NFL QB that can lead this Saints offense for the next 5 to 7 years?

Absolutely. Shough is the real deal, and his teammates rally behind him. He's been one of the few consistent bright spots for the Saints this season, along with Chris Olave, Chase Young, and Carl Granderson.

Shough showed a lot of promise in his limited starts as a rookie, then attacked the offseason and blew expectations out of the water. He's carried that same momentum into this season. His ball placement, ability to stretch the field with his arm, poise in the pocket, and ability to extend plays with his legs give the Saints a dimension offensively that they haven't really had. And most importantly, he has that clutch gene. He's shown he can make plays when the Saints need them most.

I do think there are still things he can improve on, and he's had a bit of a rough stretch over the last two weeks. Like I previously mentioned, the offense has developed a habit of starting slowly, and while I'd put more of that on Kellen Moore, Shough still plays a role in getting the offense into a rhythm. Though,gh to Shough's credit, the Saints have a nonexistent rushing attack, which hasn't eased the pressure on Shough and the passing game one bit.

But overall, I've seen more than enough to believe he can be the long-term answer in New Orleans.

As for his age, to put things into perspective, Drew Brees joined the Saints at 27 years old, and it felt like he was in New Orleans for a lifetime. Even as a 26-year-old rookie, Shough was visibly more prepared than his fellow rookies. I view his age as a benefit rather than a drawback and believe he has a long career ahead of him.

5. What is one player on the Saints offense and one player on the Saints defense that may not be a headliner that you think can make an impact on Sunday? Do you have a prediction for the game?

After the Saints invested a fourth-round pick in him last year to pry him away from Denver, Vele has come in and become the contested-catch slot receiver the Saints have been searching for since Michael Thomas.

He's quickly become one of Tyler Shough's favorite targets and has established himself as the go-to guy in third-and-long situations.

With Jordyn Tyson back in the mix alongside Chris Olave, Vele's production has dwindled a bit over the past two games. That said, I believe his opportunities will come, and he's proven he can take advantage of them.

For a six-foot-five receiver to move the way he does and possess that type of body control is truly remarkable.

On defense, keep an eye on second-year defensive back, Jonas Sanker. He consistently finds his way to the football and has been impressive in his first year starting in the slot. He returned a fumble for a touchdown against the Vikings and had another return for a touchdown in Week 1 against the Lions that was called back after Jared Goff apparently "intended" to throw the football forward.

With Justin Reid now out of the picture in New Orleans, perhaps we see Sanker move back to his natural position at safety, where he spent his rookie season.

#2915. Giants are Colts' next opponent, and John Harbaugh is taken aback by Daniel Jones news

Giants coach John Harbaugh is preparing right now for Sunday’s game against the Jaguars. But a week later, he’ll be coaching against the Colts. And he was taken by surprise today when he found out that the Colts have cut quarterback Daniel Jones.

Harbaugh was visibly surprised at his press conference today when a reporter informed him that Jones has been released, and he said when asked about the work his advance scouts are doing on the Colts that it will certainly change things.

“Yeah, probably pretty dramatically,” Harbaugh said. “I mean, wow. I don’t want to get ahead of ourselves here. You just hit me with that.”

Harbaugh said the Giants will still watch plenty of tape of the Colts’ offense this season, but they’ll also have to watch tape of Anthony Richardson.

“The offense will be the offense they run, but it will be centered around the quarterback, for sure,” Harbaugh said. “And they’re probably different styles to some degree, so we’ll have to look at that when the time comes. We have people doing that this week. They’ll have to make a left turn, obviously. Thanks for the info.”

Richardson is a great athlete with a big arm, and the Colts loved him coming out of Florida, and he’ll now have until the end of the season to show whether he can in any way resemble the quarterback the Colts thought they were getting when they took him fourth overall in the 2023 NFL draft. Harbaugh’s Giants will be the first defense Richardson faces for his first meaningful playing time in two years.

#2914. JJ Redick Gives Two Reasons For Replacing Jake LaRavia With Ziaire Williams On Lakers’ Starting Lineup

The Los Angeles Lakers play the Sacramento Kings tonight at the Crypto.Com Arena. Head coach JJ Redick once again replaced Jake LaRavia with Ziaire Williams in the Lakers' starting lineup.

Ahead of tonight's game, JJ Redick spoke to the media and explained the decision that initially puzzled many Lakers fans when they made it in their 97-109 loss to the Jazz, which ended their nine-game winning streak.

"I felt like, with the group they were going to start, again, we talked to Ziaire and Jake when we were in Milwaukee last week about both of those guys having some flexibility."

"I think Ziaire was in a really good rhythm, and he's had some good games since he missed some time. We were trying to get him back into a rhythm. He hasn't really had one over the last three games or so. So, some of it was about that, and some of it was about the matchup."

"Those guys are both easy, low-maintenance winners. They're not going to be affected by that. I think the message from day one of training camp has always been that every game is going to be a little different. It's going to present itself differently, and the challenges are going to be different. You have to play the game in front of you, and the guys are bought into that," Redick explained.

Before tonight, Jake LaRavia averaged 5.8 points, 4.1 rebounds, and 2.2 assists while shooting 47.1% from the field and 46.7% from beyond the arc so far this season.

Ziaire Williams averaged 9.5 points, 2.4 rebounds, and 1.8 assists while shooting 56.4% from the field and 44.7% from beyond the arc.

It seemed like Jake LaRavia had cemented his role in the Lakers' starting lineup in 15 games (11 starts). But Ziaire Williams clearly stepped up and made a promising case for a starter role (six starts in 13 games played).

The Lakers began the season with three prominently fixed starters whenever they are healthy: Luka Doncic, Austin Reaves, and Walker Kessler. As a result, the wings were always a position to contend for and earn as the season progressed.

Given that both power forwards are willing to do whatever it takes to win, this is a good problem for the Lakers: everyone plays so well that they don't know who to start or who can come off the bench and make an impact.

They seem likely to use this position rotationally throughout the season, based on the matchups they expect. It will be interesting to see who the Lakers' coaching staff decides to start as the playoffs approach later in the season.

But down the stretch, it remains to be seen who earns the regular starting power-forward role, as role clarity helps players mold their tendencies accordingly.

#2913. Dominick Barlow probable, Jaylen Brown out for Sixers vs. Bucks

Reinforcements are finally on their way for the Sixers. Dominick Barlow appears ready to make his season debut, as he's listed as probable for Saturday's game against the Milwaukee Bucks.

Tony Jones of The Athletic reported earlier Friday that Barlow was trending in that direction, and Barlow foreshadowed it on Instagram on Thursday as well. Still, for a Sixers team that's been woefully short on frontcourt depth as of late, his return could not be coming at a better time.

Even with Barlow back, the Sixers won't be at full strength against Milwaukee. Adem Bona (cervical spine strain), Jaylen Brown (hamstring) and Dean Wade (hamstring) are all out, while Tyrese Maxey is listed as questionable with a right foot sprain. For the Bucks, Ousmane Dieng (left finger fracture) is their only notable player who's out.

Luckily for the Sixers, the Bucks only loosely resemble a professional basketball team this year as they begin to chart a course in the post-Giannis Antetokoumpo era. They're an NBA-worst 1-15, and this will be their third game in four days. The 1 p.m. ET start likely won't do their tired legs any favors.

Since this will be Barlow's first game back this season, the Sixers figure to ease him in slowly. It'd be surprising if he played more than 15-20 minutes at most. Still, it'll bear watching how the Sixers deploy him. Will he stick strictly to the 4, or will he cut into Tacko Time and steal some minutes at backup center whenever Joel Embiid is off the floor?

Barlow was a revelation for the Sixers last year, averaging 7.7 points and 4.8 rebounds in 23.8 minutes per game while shooting 53.9 percent from the field. They initially signed him to a two-way contact last offseason, but he played so well for them—he even started 59 games!—that they wound up spending a hefty chunk of their taxpayer mid-level exception to convert him to a standard contract after the trade deadline.

Barlow's three-point shooting might be the swing skill that determines his place in the Sixers' rotation this year. He knocked down only 25.6 percent of his long-range attempts on low volume last season, which won't cut it moving forward. Jabari Walker's improvement from deep gives him the inside track to backup frontcourt minutes, but it's worth seeing how a Walker-Barlow pairing fares defensively, particularly with regard to rim protection.

The Sixers proved Wednesday that they're capable of winning even with Brown, Wade and Maxey out. Regardless of whether Maxey returns, they should be able to take care of business against an overmatched Bucks team. But Barlow's return adds some extra juice to an otherwise sleepy 1 p.m. ET start against a lottery-bound squad.

#2912. Colts officially place Daniel Jones on waivers

Daniel Jones is officially on the waiver wire.

The NFL transaction report has gone out this afternoon, and Jones appears as a waiver from the Colts. After the trade deadline, every player who gets released by his team has to go on waivers before he becomes a free agent.

That means any team that wants Jones on his current contract can claim him, but that is extraordinarily unlikely to happen. As the waiver transaction notes, Jones is a vested veteran with a fully guaranteed salary for the rest of this season. No team is going to want to claim that salary.

Once he clears waivers, however, he'll be an unrestricted free agent, free to sign with any team, and it won't be surprising if some team wants to add him for veteran depth. Jones has been terrible this season, but he's been a competent starter at times in his career, and a team that wants an experienced backup may be interested in signing him for a minimal salary.

Jones was also waived almost exactly two years ago, on November 22, 2024. He cleared waivers and signed with the Vikings' practice squad before signing with the Colts after that season.

#2911. AMD dives on short seller report of alleged China exposure

Shares of chipmaker Advanced Micro Devices close down 7.72% to $489.19 on Thursday after investment firm Hunterbrook Media issued a report alleging that the company "has its own China problem."

Hunterbrook disclosed that it had taken a short position in AMD.

The report claims that "after the U.S. blacklisted Chinese server maker IEIT Systems, the company kept getting newer AMD firmware, which enables AMD chips to run in servers."

CNBC did not independently verify this information.

Hunterbrook also said that new export rules in Washington could "tighten restrictions on AMD's foreign business," alleging that AMD "bills over 20% of its revenue to customers in China and Hong Kong," and "depends on a Chinese partner to finish most of its chips."

The allegations come amid investigations of rival chipmaker Nvidia over its sales to companies in Asia with ties to China. AMD has been viewed as a beneficiary of the uncertainty surrounding Nvidia.

"The claims misrepresent AMD's business practices and relationships," an AMD spokesperson said in a statement. "AMD complies with applicable US export control laws and regulations and maintains rigorous processes to ensure compliance across our global operations. We remain focused on serving our customers and upholding the highest standards of integrity."

Lisa Su, CEO of AMD, told investors on the company's recent earnings call that China is an "important" market and that the semiconductor giant wants to be fully compliant with U.S. export controls.

AMD continued to slide after hours, trading down 0.45% to $487 shortly after 4 p.m.

#2910. Magic star Franz Wagner talks finally playing against brother Moe in win over Nets

Magic star Franz Wagner talks finally playing against brother Moe in win over Nets appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Orlando Magic escaped the Brooklyn Nets with a 113-111 NBA Cup victory on Friday night, and for Franz Wagner, the night carried a little extra meaning. Not only did the Magic forward hit the game-winner, but he also faced off against his older brother, Moritz "Moe" Wagner, in an official NBA game for the very first time.

The two brothers spent five seasons together in Orlando before Moe signed a two-year, $19 million deal with the Nets in free agency this past summer. That meant Friday's matchup was the first time the Wagners ever shared the floor as opponents in a game that counted.

After the game, Franz admitted that it felt strange to finally see his brother on the other side.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

"Yes, it was. Yeah, first time," Franz said, via Darko Dzeletovic. "Obviously we played basketball one-on-one together or against each other. Obviously in practices, you know, we played for five years together. But, you know, he's four years older, so I never matched up in an actual game. So, yeah, I think he was 29 and I was 25. So, obviously we played basketball for a long time, so that made it extra weird."

On the court, the 25-year-old was brilliant from start to finish, finishing with 35 points, five rebounds and seven assists on 13-of-25 shooting. He repeatedly attacked the basket throughout the night and delivered the decisive bucket when the Magic needed it most.

Orlando also got a big lift from Desmond Bane, who knocked down seven 3-pointers en route to 21 points to go along with five steals. Nikola Vucevic added 15 points and eight rebounds on a perfect 5-of-5 shooting.

For the Nets, Julius Randle led the way with 27 points and nine rebounds, while Danny Wolf scored 20 points off four triples. Rookie Mikel Brown Jr. chipped in 18 points, and Day'Ron Sharpe posted a double-double with 17 points and 11 rebounds.

Franz also noted that the NBA Cup brings out a different level of motivation for the Magic.

"In these games, obviously we play for a little bit of extra motivation," Franz said. "We obviously try to win every game, but I think there's a little bit of something different about the games."

#2908. Brandon Ingram expected to make season debut soon with Clippers

Brandon Ingram is set to make his season debut with the Los Angeles Clippers on Monday, Nov. 23, according to ESPN's Shams Charania.

The Clippers will play the Houston Rockets at the Toyota Center in Houston on Monday.

Ingram had been working his way back from right heel surgery he had in May. The two-time All-Star also had a partially torn Achilles tendon.

Ingram was initially supposed to have a bone removed from his heel, but the doctors found the partially torn Achilles during that procedure.

Earlier this month, Ingram told reporters that he was "pretty close" to returning and had been on the court a bit more.

He also stated that he had not played any 5-on-5 during the summer but has since been "gaining that confidence."

The Clippers acquired Ingram as one of the primary trade assets in the package sent over by the Toronto Raptors for Kawhi Leonard during the offseason.

Ingram has remain close with the team since his arrival.

He took some time to praise his teammates despite the Clippers' slow start to the season. L.A. has a 4-10 record through the first 14 games.

"I actually want to commend those guys because everybody is in new positions and asked to do more," Ingram told reporters earlier this month. "So the guys are trying. They're playing really, really good defense."

The Clippers fell short in a 124-112 loss to the San Antonio Spurs, in what was a competitive game considering both teams are on opposite sides of the standings.

Ingram return would be welcomed, especially on the offensive end of the floor. The All-Star led Toronto with 21.5 points and was third in rebounds (5.6) during the 2025-26 season. He started 77 games for the Raptors.

"They're trying to figure it out on the offensive end," Ingram told reporters. "When I come in, I want to play make, make it a little bit easier for guys to catch and shoot, and not have to make so many decisions. And be able to, when it gets down to it, be able to score."

Ingram already had a level of familiarity with head coach Tyronn Lue and assistant coach Jeff Van Gundy from playing for Team USA during the 2023 FIBA World Cup.

The Clippers had several players injured to start the month, including Ingram, center Yanic Konan Niederhäuser and Max Strus.

The trio was recalled on Nov. 2 and participated in a weekend assignment with the team's G-League affiliate.

Strus returned to the team after recovering from a partial tear to his right plantar fascia. Konan Niederhäuser made his season debut on Nov. 19 against the Clippers, after recovering from right Lisfranc surgery.

Bradley Beal (right knee injury management) and Jordan Miller (left rotator cuff surgery) will remain the only Clippers without a timetable to return.

#2907. Anthropic was supposed to save the AI trade. What went wrong?

Anthropic's historic IPO — the largest in U.S. history — delivered a big win for the company and its investors. But it failed at another task: reviving animal spirits in the AI trade.

The Nasdaq Composite closed down 2.25% amid a broader market pullback with the S&P 500 declining 2% to 7,175.

Investors had been looking forward to the Anthropic IPO as a "clearing event" — reviving the tech sector after months of choppy trading, the Nvidia espionage scandal, and growing concerns that AI chip sales to Asia are likely to slump as Washington cracks down on potential violations of export controls.

Some of those worries resurfaced Thursday as a short seller, Hunterbrook Media, alleged in a report that firmware sold by chipmaker AMD had turned up in U.S.-blacklisted companies. AMD stock closed down more than 7%. The chipmaker told CNBC it complies with U.S. law.

Adding to the worries, the House Foreign Affairs Committee released documents showing that a subagency of the Commerce Department approved the sale of more than 879,000 AI chips to Chinese companies. That also raised the prospect of an Asia sales slowdown under stricter export controls.

Many investors and analysts continue to express confidence in the AI trade, noting that Anthropic's successful market debut — the stock closed up 17% from its IPO price and raised more than $81 billion — bodes well for capital spending across tech.

But investors acknowledged that this is a high-tension moment and said it's time to be more strategic about the trade.

Dan Niles, founder of Niles Investment Management, described himself as a "circumspect bull," making the comparison to market conditions in 2022 when the big indices tanked in the back half of the year.

"You could have still made money that year. You just needed to have the right combination of longs and shorts," he said.

Part of the challenge, Niles said, has been a price war between the two cutting-edge frontier labs, Anthropic and OpenAI. That has posed a threat to the overall revenue and profit potential of AI and the capex going into it. Fierce competition on price is producing collective losses between the two top companies, he notes.

"The combined revenue growth for OpenAI plus Anthropic went from growing 75% sequentially in Q2 to 48% in Q3," he told CNBC. "The combined losses went from $7.9 billion to about $8.4 billion from Q2 to Q3."

While uncertainty is high now, some resolution may be on the horizon if a greater degree of commercial and regulatory clarity emerges over the next few months.

Trading experts also told CNBC that the tech sector's troubles Thursday were a more serious matter than Anthropic simply siphoning capital from other parts of the market.

"There's a reset of AI in general," said JJ Kinahan, senior vice president at Cboe Global Markets.

Investors for years have profited in tech by buying the dip, he said, but "there's been an attitude change that [you] can't buy things blindly and they'll all go up." Nvidia's ongoing declines have shaken confidence in the buy-the-dip strategy, he said.

Anthropic's next few trading days will be a key gauge of the market's strength. Friday will be a "wild card," Kinahan said. It could be a "final flush out" for the market, or a bounce that leaves the market in "no man's land" while volatility remains elevated.

A better gauge may be Monday, when options on Anthropic start trading, providing insight into whether retail investors will back the company in force — providing more of a vote of confidence in the AI trade.

"If people think we're going up [on Anthropic] they'll buy stock, but retail particularly will just buy calls," he said. "There's a bigger test coming Monday."

For now, investors continue to sell AI-related stocks.

Neoclouds, the generally highly indebted AI-dedicated computing providers at the outer edges of the AI trade, were down substantially Thursday, with CoreWeave closing down more than 6%.

Hyperscalers such as Alphabet and Amazon.com, viewed as relatively defensive AI bets, also finished lower.

One bright spot: Cybersecurity stocks continued to rally, with Palo Alto Networks and CrowdStrike both closing up. AI is finding security holes across software infrastructure, creating a scramble to patch the vulnerabilities before hackers can exploit them.

— CNBC's Daren Fonda contributed reporting.
