#2904. Reitz girls basketball wins season opener against North

It took some time before Reitz girls basketball began its season.

The Panthers have been battling it out with each other throughout the last month during practice. But then came November 20 — the season opener against North.

More: South Knox girls basketball out to prove it is one of the top teams in Indiana

Reitz would have the opportunity to showcase its extended preparation in front of its home crowd. On the other hand, the Huskies came into the night having already played three games.

The Panthers' lack of game action showed itself, but not enough to put the outcome in North's favor. After Reitz led by just one point (43-42) entering the fourth quarter, coach Brock Stone's group pulled away with a 25-14 effort in the final frame to win 68-56.

Panthers guard/forward Kierstyn Kaiser led the way with 27 points. The junior showcased an ability to finish inside and a willingness to make plays for others.

Freshman guard Molly Swedenburg was big down the stretch. Seven of her eight points came in the fourth quarter.

And while there were lapses in concentration, the Reitz defense created the separation. Several turnovers were created because of the Panthers' ball pressure putting North in tough positions to escape.

It was a win in the first game, but head coach Brock Stone certainly was not satisfied with the performance.

"I don't think we played up to the standard that we try to uphold," Stone said. "I was disappointed in how we guarded the ball and how we rebounded. In the past, that's (been) part of who we are."

Stone acknowledged the schedule playing a factor in how his team played. The rustiness was inevitable against an SIAC opponent that has been playing since the first week of November.

"I don't like the way our schedule is set up," Stone said. "When you haven't been able to play a game, but then you come out against a team that our girls have grown up with their whole lives, it's just something I've got to fix in the offseason this year to get us some games so that we're ready for those moments."

While it might have affected its readiness for the opener, long term, Stone is not worried about his team's ability to meet the standard. In the fourth quarter, what he hopes to see more of was on display.

Reitz created several multi-possession leads, but North always had a response. Well, up until the fourth quarter.

North is now 2-2 after the loss on November 20. First-year coach Brad Elliot felt his group was overwhelmed by Kaiser in the fourth quarter. The junior scored 14 of her 27 points in the final frame.

"Kaiser taking it to the basket really got us," Elliott said. "Her length got us there at the end."

Elliott felt his team did not make it tough enough for Kaiser on those drives into the paint. Rather than making her earn it at the line, there were a few soft fouls that resulted in and-one opportunities.

With Kaiser's size advantage, he would have liked for his team to anticipate her habits. Instead, they found it difficult to stay in front of the junior and secure defensive rebounds.

Still, Elliott was proud of North's resilience. Senior guard Madelynn Walters led the Huskies with 13 points.

North will be at Castle (0-5) on November 24. Reitz has a quick turnaround — playing the second half of a back-to-back on November 20 at home against New Albany (1-3).

Markos Tsegaye is a sports reporter for the Courier & Press. He can be reached at mtsegaye@usatodayco.com. Follow him on X @10_Markos_.

#2903. Things look bleak for Warriors, but they can survive this

The Warriors are in free fall, losing five games in a row, including their last four by 20 or more points.

Golden State Warriors guard Stephen Curry, right, confers with head coach Steve Kerr during an NBA game in Denver, Monday, Oct. 26, 2026. AP Photo/David Zalubowski

Of the 15 Western Conference teams, only 11 are going to have a real shot at securing the 10 playoff spots unless something drastically changes.

In other words, the West cellar is so weak that even if the Warriors can't arrest their precipitous slide without Steph Curry, they'll have more than enough time to recover when he returns.

So, while what's happening to the Warriors is unfortunate, it's far from catastrophic.

Curry is expected to be sidelined close to a month after suffering a right oblique abdominal tear on Nov. 11.

The 38-year-old had carried the Warriors to eight straight wins while playing some of the best basketball of his career, averaging 27.8 points (50% shooting from the field, 45.7% from beyond the arc), 3.7 rebounds and 3.9 assists per game.

Before Curry sustained the injury, he and Yaxel Lendeborg were looking like one of the NBA's best duos. Lendeborg, whom the Warriors drafted with the No. 11 overall pick in June, was having a Rookie of the Year campaign, averaging 21.6 points on 52.2% shooting.

But with defenses collapsing on him without Curry, the rookie has wilted, averaging 13.7 points on 26.8% shooting over the last four games.

Not only is Curry going to be back in the lineup soon, but Jimmy Butler (torn right ACL) is expected to return midseason.

The Warriors could still be really good. They could still have a deep playoff run. They could still be a contender.

This has been a roller coaster for Warriors fans.

Entering the season, it was questionable whether the Warriors would make the playoffs after they pretty much stood pat during the offseason. And after dropping their first three games, it seemed all but guaranteed that this was going to be a wasted season for Curry, a two-time MVP who carried the Warriors to four championships in eight years from 2015-2022.

Lendeborg emerged as his sidekick. He sprinted past his expectations. He became a force.

Yaxel Lendeborg, left, and Golden State Warriors head coach Steve Kerr smile during a game against the Houston Rockets on Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026, in Houston. NBAE via Getty Images

All of a sudden, Curry and Lendeborg were stomping on their competition, beating contenders such as the Lakers, Timberwolves and Nuggets over a stunning stretch that had pundits questioning whether the Warriors were championship contenders.

But the short-handed Warriors were always one Curry injury away from disaster.

And when their nightmare was realized, the Warriors went from being one of the league's most fun teams to one of its biggest duds.

Golden State Warriors head coach Steve Kerr watches play during an NBA game against the San Antonio Spurs on Friday, Nov. 13, 2026, in San Antonio. AP Photo/Darren Abate

Curry and Butler will return, and the Warriors will have more than enough runway to correct course.

So the excitement the team was generating a little over a week ago?

#2902. Cathie Wood’s Ark Invest was a big buyer of Anthropic as she tells CNBC she’s ‘quite optimistic’

Cathie Wood was a major buyer of Anthropic following its debut Thursday on the Nasdaq, adding shares in all her major funds.

Wood's firm, which already owned private shares of the artificial intelligence company in its Ark Venture Fund leading up to the initial public offering, bought $90.8 million worth of Anthropic in six funds, according to a trade notification from the firm.

"We've owned Anthropic since early '23," Wood told CNBC's "Squawk Box" on Friday. "It's our best performing equity by far in ARKVX, our venture fund. ... Our confidence has grown with time as it really took over the enterprise" AI business.

Anthropic shares jumped 17% in their first day, but closed well off the highs of the session and were headed for sizable losses Friday. The Nasdaq, which was expected to get a boost from a major IPO reviving enthusiasm about the technology trade, fell by 2% on Thursday.

"We're climbing a wall of worry," said Wood. "Everybody is worried about AI hype and the political situation after the election, but I think we'll look back and say, OK this is more like the '80s and '90s, climbing a wall of worry you know with bricks much like what we have today: inflation, interest rates, wars, new technologies and so forth so quite optimistic here."

The Anthropic stakes are relatively small across the funds, but Wood typically will buy shares in companies she likes steadily during long stretches. Wood said she is "averaging down" with her purchases of Anthropic.

In her $6.1 billion Ark Innovation ETF, Anthropic is a 0.3% stake. The biggest holdings in that fund are Tesla and SpaceX, representing more than 21% of the fund together.

Ark Innovation, which retail investors piled into during the pandemic as it gained about 150% in 2020, tumbled the subsequent next two years, losing 67% in 2022 alone. Wood's following was dented after the big losses.

The fund had a comeback stretch as the bull market revved back up from 2023 to 2025, but has fallen on hard times again this year, down more than 10% in 2026. The S&P 500 is up 4.8% this year.

Wood dismissed comparisons of today's environment to the dot-com bubble.

"Back then if an Anthropic had gone public, it probably would have gone up threefold the first day," she said. "That's how crazy things were."

#2900. As Anthropic shares tank, investors scramble to hedge. It could be very expensive

As Anthropic shares tumble toward their IPO price of $83, investors are looking for ways to hedge. Unfortunately, they may have to wait until Monday, when options on the AI giant are expected to list.

If Anthropic breaks its IPO price, it will represent a 27% decline from Thursday's intraday high of $113. The wild price swings offer clues on how expensive it might be to buy protection on the new tech giant once options begin trading.A 20% intraday move points to an implied volatility somewhere between 100 and 150 for Anthropic options, according to estimates from single-stock market-makers on the Cboe trading floor in Chicago.

Options traders are also looking at how SpaceX traded in the days following its offering for clues on how options might price.

Implied volatility in SpaceX started at 116, but that didn't deter options traders big and small from piling into the contracts tracking the stock, with about 1.7 million contracts traded on the first day for a total options premium of about $2.5 billion. Far out-of-the-money call contracts were some of the most popular trades as the stock soared 40% in its first three sessions.One key difference between now and then? Anthropic is trading against the backdrop of S&P 500 volatility close to 30, compared to 17.3 when SpaceX debuted. "I expect ANT options to be super active," said Tom Sosnoff, co-founder of the trading platforms ThinkOrSwim and TastyTrade. "I expect heavy options volume."One thing Anthropic and SpaceX have in common is heavy retail involvement in both IPOs, with retail brokerages receiving a 20% allocation from SpaceX and reports pointing to between a 15-20% allocation from Anthropic on Thursday. "Round one of the Anthropic IPO easily goes to the individual investor," Sosnoff added. "Retail participants bailed early at the best prices of the day while institutional investors paid up to top off their underwhelming allocations. I believe retail was aggressively reloading in the mid-90s."Further signs of retail demand will become evident when options likely begin trading on Monday. One thing to watch: the spread between out-of-the-money call and put contracts, which was heavily skewed toward calls in the case of SpaceX. "I would be careful comparing Anthropic to SpaceX," said Dennis Davitt, CIO and Co-Founder of Millbank Dartmoor Portsmouth. "From a market-structure perspective, the two are dramatically different. The availability and cost of borrowing will be different, the companies are at very different stages of maturity, and SpaceX has allowed employees to monetize portions of their holdings over time. All of those factors matter when pricing options."

#2898. Jimmy Garoppolo’s reunion may not be with the Rams

If the Los Angeles Rams lost Matthew Stafford for any amount of time this season, would you trust the current backup quarterback options?

Would you feel any better if an experienced veteran, like Jimmy Garoppolo, could step in?

There's nothing to indicate Stafford is at any increased risk of an injury or missing snaps for any reason. But as the NFL playoffs approach and the margin for error shrinks closer to zero, it's hard not to think about protecting yourself against calamity. That's especially true for a Rams team that has dealt with tons of injuries this year, including another serious one to a starting offensive lineman just this week.

This is relevant now because Garoppolo, who spent the 2024 and 2025 seasons in Los Angeles, is suddenly being linked to other playoff contenders, like another one of his former teams, the New England Patriots.

After initial reporting on the matter by Jordan Schultz, ESPN's Jeremy Fowler discussed the situation this week and threw some cold water on the topic but stopped short of completely killing the idea. Specifically, he mentioned that there are questions about whether or not the 12-year veteran has any actual desire to play again.

He also mentioned, however, that Garoppolo enjoyed playing on the west coast. That could work in the Rams' favor, obviously.

Don't get it twisted, this is not an earth-shattering kind of hypothetical personnel scenario. This is a quarterback who had good moments in several years as a starter, but isn't exactly a Hall of Fame candidate. He would purely be an insurance policy if Los Angeles wanted to pursue that route. And realistically, if Stafford missed more than a game or a couple games at the absolute most, things would get dire in a hurry for the Rams.

Still, the alternatives currently are Stetson Bennett, who's taken a whopping 10 offensive snaps over four seasons (all of which have come this year) and Ty Simpson, the first-round pick who still hasn't seen the field and who is a known project player.

This idea isn't completely out of left field, either, as it was discussed over the offseason as the backup job was still in flux in the wake of the Simpson pick in the NFL draft. There isn't much that's controversial about re-signing a generally inoffensive backup quarterback.

The Rams have enough personnel concerns that they likely don't have to go full bore after another reserve quarterback right now. But if push came to shove and they had to sign one out of desperation, Garoppolo still makes sense. That's the situation the Patriots are quite literally in right now with Drake Maye injured.

It would be a terrible scenario regardless, but a slightly less devastating one with a more reliable backup in the fray.

(8-2) defeated CREC, 41-6 at New Britain, to earn a spot in Class S.

The CIAC playoff qualifier projections are determined using the CIAC playoff formula, using OpenAI's GPT-6 Luna medium model and verified by GametimeCT using the current CIAC football playoff standings and formula.

All qualifiers remain unofficial until the CIAC's football committee meets the day after the regular season concludes on Thanksgiving morning. The playoffs begin Tuesday, Dec. 1 at the sites of the top-four seeds in each of the six divisions.

#2895. Prediction markets outlook for stocks in 2026 dims amid AI sell-off

As stocks continue their tech-driven sell-off, prediction markets traders are seeing a bleaker outlook for the remainder of the year.

On platform Kalshi, speculators place just 6% odds that the S&P 500 will cross 8,000 in 2026. On Oct. 13, those odds were as high as 72%.

Contracts ask traders if the S&P 500 will go above various levels in 2026, and they're resolved using data from S&P Dow Jones Indices.

On Friday, the Nasdaq Composite turned negative for the year as investors continued to dump tech stocks, primarily those with exposure to the artificial intelligence build-out. The Dow Jones Industrial Average and the S&P 500 fell into correction territory, too.

The S&P 500 has slashed its year-to-date gain to just over 2%.

The Stock Trader's Almanac in a Thursday note broadly agreed that the hopes for a December rally are fading, with the caveat that a "Santa rally" could still appear at the end of the year.

"The market is under pressure and more downside can't be ruled out," the note said.

The Stock Trader's Almanac sees 2026 following the year-end pattern of 2018, when the Federal Reserve was in a tightening cycle in a midterm year. The Fed is widely expected to hike rates at its December meeting.

In that scenario, the Stock Trader's Almanac thinks more churn is to come. "The low is more likely in mid- to late December than this week."

#2894. Fan dies after falling during a fight at 49ers-Cowboys game

A man who attended Sunday’s game between the 49ers and Cowboys has died due to injuries sustained in a fight during the game.

Via The Independent, 36-year-old Marco Narro was involved in an altercation with another fan near kickoff to the game. During the fight, Narro fell. After the incident, Narro stayed and watched the game.

During the game, he started to complain about pain. He and a friend sought attention from paramedics after the game. Narro was then transported to a local hospital.

On Wednesday, Narro died. An autopsy is pending.

The Arlington Police Department has opened a formal investigation. The prevalence of security cameras at AT&T Stadium should be useful when it comes to reconstructing the events.

The situation underscores one of the basic risks of engaging in fights at sporting events. It doesn’t take much to suffer, or to inflict, a very serious injury.

#2892. No. 8 Pomperaug football rallies past Notre Dame Prep in OT behind Jack Guinipero

FAIRFIELD - The Pomperaug football team has been stomping all over opponents with a relentless rushing attack this season.

But senior Jack Guinipero made one thing clear after the team's latest game.

"We can pass, too," he said with a laugh.

Guinipero threw a game-tying touchdown pass to Caleb McKee with 51 seconds remaining, then ran for an 8-yard score in overtime to give No. 8-ranked Pomperaug a dramatic 27-20 victory over Notre Dame Prep on Friday at Sacred Heart University's Campus Field.

Guinipero even had a hand, literally, on the final play when he knocked away a fourth-down pass from Notre Dame Prep's Jordan Razor.

But the tying pass, on a play Guinipero called "28 Rocket Pass," was the biggest highlight, with Guinipero firing a pass from the sidelines into the end zone, and McKee going up under double coverage to make the catch.

"That's something we've had on the back burner forever," Guinipero said. "It's one of those plays where, we've got it in our back pocket any time we need it. I hit my favorite receiver, Caleb McKee, an absolute dog and he went over two people for it. Beast, I love to see it."

While the pass was the highlight, the running game did the heavy lifting as usual, with Guinipero rushing for 119 yards and a touchdown, Dom Creaco rushing for 80 yards and two touchdowns, and Josh Coughlin rushing for 97 yards.

But the Panthers had to overcome three turnovers: two fumbles and an interception by Notre Dame Prep's Razor.

"What I loved about this was we overcame that," Pomperaug coach Tom Pereira said. "We kept fighting and fighting, held them out for a two (point attempt) to keep the game within reach, and then we made plays down the stretch. I challenged our defense before this game. Aside from that series coming out of the halftime, we really bottled up their running game and forced them to have to go vertically down the field."

The game was the regular-season finale for both teams, which had already unofficially secured playoff berths.

Pomperaug (9-1) will be making its first trip to the state tournament since finishing runner-up in the Class L championship in 2009. The Panthers are currently second in Class MM and will likely host a quarterfinal game.

Notre Dame Prep (8-2), which is in Class M, is back in the playoffs after missing the cut last season, but Friday's loss may have cost it a shot at a home playoff game. The Lancers are now sixth in Class M.

"Going into the playoffs, this is the type of game you want," Notre Dame Prep coach John Johnson said. "This was the most physical team we've played all year long and our guys fought the entire game. We got put in situations where you're thinking, do we go for two? Do we kick it? Do we go to overtime? All those things that can pop up in the playoffs when it's do or die time popped up tonight so this is a really good learning tool."

Pomperaug was chasing Notre Dame Prep for much of the second half.

Ja'cari Harris scored on a 24-yard run in the third quarter to put the Lancers ahead, and quarterback JJ Studeny connected with Marvel Johnson on the conversion pass for a 14-7 lead.

The Panthers answered with an 80-yard drive that ended with a 2-yard touchdown run by Creaco, his second of the game. Notre Dame stuffed the conversion run, keeping its one-point lead at 14-13 with 9:07 remaining.

Harris, who had 111 rushing yards for the Lancers, opened the next possession with a 33-yard run and set up an eventual 10-yard touchdown by Studeny for a 20-13 lead with 6:57 on the clock. Notre Dame Prep went for two to try to make it a nine-point game, but the pass failed.

That led to another long drive by the Panthers and Guinipero's touchdown pass to McKee in the final minute.

Guinipero then scored on an 8-yard run in overtime, and Pomperaug shut down the Lancers for the win.

Jack Guinipero, Pomperaug: The senior was everywhere. He threw the game-tying touchdown pass, rushed for the game-winning touchdown, recovered a fumble and had 119 rushing yards overall.

"It all started back, even three years ago, Thanksgiving, right after Notre Dame Prep my sophomore year. We started up that real winter lifting program. We lifted 24-7, 365 (days a year), and we're conditioned for situations like this. We can dig deep and come out on top no matter what."- Pomperaug's Jack Guinipero

#2888. Where the 49ers need to improve defensively vs. Vikings

Last weekend was not a good day for the San Francisco 49ers defense, as they gave up 40 points on the Dallas Cowboys, yet still managed to come away with the 46-40 victory.

San Francisco's defense struggled on all fronts. The secondary especially had its issues, as Dallas's top three receivers all had quality games. George Pickens had 11 catches for 145 yards and three touchdowns, and that was with him dropping three passes. CeeDee Lamb had seven catches for 72 yards and a touchdown, while Ryan Flournoy had six catches for 71 yards and a touchdown.

Cornerbacks Deommodore Lenoir, Renardo Green, and Upton Stout entered the game banged up, and it showed, as both had their fair share of struggles against Dallas's elite offense. Of course, the matchup wasn't easy facing a high-caliber quarterback with two Pro Bowl receivers, but it was a rough day for a defense that had been quite good over the first half of the season.

That wasn't the only issue. The 49ers pass rush was also ineffective, failing to sack Dak Prescott, and Nick Bosa came out this week and took accountability for his play this season. Bosa has yet to record a sack, although his other numbers still indicate a productive season, especially with the double teams and chips he's facing.

Now, this week, the health is looking better. Stout remained limited in practice with his ongoing shoulder injury and is questionable, but Lenoir wasn't on the injury report and Renardo Green practiced in full on Thursday and is off the injury report for Sunday's game.

But the 49ers still have a tough challenge against the Minnesota Vikings, who put up 30 points on the Green Bay Packers in Kirk Cousins's return. Minnesota's offense suddenly looks formidable, and this battle should be a tough one on both sides.

After their struggles against Dallas, where do the 49ers need to improve defensively against Minnesota? Ahead of Sunday night, defensive coordinator Raheem Morris broke down the main issues he saw from the Cowboys game and what to take from it.

"Extending drives early with some of the penalties to get those guys to go down there, particularly on the first drive [was a problem]. We gave up three, I believe, on that first one," Morris said. "We got the big penalty to get those guys down there. They got a big-time field goal kicker. A couple of big plays in the first half. The two big plays were the ones that killed us there early. Got a pretty good grip on it after that and kind of held it down. It was at some point it was like 38-19. Then that finishing moments, we didn't do a very good job of getting the finishes off. Usually, the finishes happen when you're able to generate pressure with sacks or you're able to get interceptions.

"The Cowboys did a nice job, to give them credit, of getting the ball out of their hand. Did a nice job of getting the ball into their good players' hand, keeping drives going, extending drives. They had about three or four drives there where it was almost like overtime-like play for them, where it was going to be fourth down territory regardless and they were able to capitalize and go down and score on us. You would like to be able to stop some of those plays and be able to get those guys going and get off the grass at those moments and then that score wouldn't have got to where it was, but those things didn't happen.

"We didn't execute that way. And it's a collective group. I know [Nick] Bosa stood up in front of you guys and took all the credit and took all the accountability, but I really think it's just all of us. Some of it could be better play calls for them. Some of it could be better for us, what we can do for those guys. Some of it can be better plays made by us. Some of it, we just ran out a little bit of gas, got a little bit tired. Let's not kid ourselves. Human nature as well. So, fortunate to get out of there with a win. Definitely want to learn those lessons when you get a chance to do those things in those moments, when you get wins. Something that we can learn from and grow from and something that we definitely will."

One thing Morris did see from his team was accountability after the game, with the leaders on the team stepping up.

"They're such an accountable group," Morris added. "Most guys, you come in and you're just upset after a game. These guys, they hold themselves to such a high standard. They come in, they're able to speak very clearly right off the bat. So it's not going to be more vocal the next week because they were vocal the first week. Not necessarily Bosa. Bosa's kind of a to-himself guy, but for a guy like Fred [Warner], he's always going to speak up and do the things that happened. It wasn't like there was mistakes. They did some better job executing. There was a few mistakes. There's always going to be mistakes and there's always correcting mistakes. I'm not saying there's not mistakes, but there's always going to be correcting mistakes, but you correct those mistakes, you move on.

"There's some other things that, just you talking to as an individual player. The best thing about our guys, they always point at themselves first. Whether it's Fred, whether it's Bosa, whether it's DMo (Deommodore Lenoir), Renardo [Green], anybody on our football team. That's the cool part about being here with these guys is they point at themselves first. Then a lot of the times I'm able to say, 'Hey, I can help you here' or 'we can do these things better. We can make these calls. We can help you out, do a little bit better in these situations.' They are all so upfront and forthgoing with those things. That makes it really cool to talk to those guys that way."

Health seemed to be a factor last weekend, but the 49ers do need to be more disciplined and avoid the killer penalties (although some looked to be questionable calls).

But there is a lot at stake this weekend for the 49ers, as their No. 1 seed odds shift dramatically with a win or a loss. San Francisco's offense has always been up to the task, but they'll get their toughest matchup of the year with Brian Flores's defense. So, the 49ers defense will need to show up as well against this new-look Vikings offense with Kirk Cousins and execute on all three levels.

#2882. Pat Freiermuth says Steelers still believe in DK Metcalf

DK Metcalf hasn't quite lived up to expectations during his time in Pittsburgh, but that hasn't shaken the confidence of his teammates. Pat Freiermuth opened up to the media today about the embattled wideout's time in the Steel City.

"Obviously he's had some struggles but if you look at his effort on the run game or downfield blocking, he doesn't let his situation impact his ability to get other guys open," said Freiermuth on Thursday afternoon.

"I think that's something that you can appreciate as a teammate and as a guy you can tell a lot about his character. You know, we're with him until the end. He's going to get this figured out. We've got a lot of trust in DK and a lot of belief in him. We paid him for a reason, and the Steelers have backed him for a reason."

Metcalf's struggles have been a combination of costly drops, difficulty creating separation against physical coverage and inconsistency in the Steelers' passing game. Opposing defenses have increasingly focused on limiting his opportunities, while Pittsburgh has leaned heavily on its running game.

The Steelers acquired Metcalf to be their No. 1 receiving threat, and his physical tools and big-play ability remain undeniable. He previously established himself as one of the NFL's more productive receivers in Seattle, recording 6,324 receiving yards and 48 touchdowns from 2019-2024.

While the production hasn't consistently followed him to Pittsburgh, Freiermuth's confidence suggests the locker room still believes Metcalf can turn things around. The challenge now is translating his talent into more consistent results on the field.

#2879. Epic Early Season Matchup as Hurricanes host Edmonton: Preview, How to Watch, Game Thread

After only playing two hockey games over the span of eleven days thanks to the trip to Finland, the Carolina Hurricanes now sprint into their second game in three days. It's also the first of a back-to-back to make it three in four. Fortunately for Carolina they only have to face one of two teams ahead of them in points—though not points percentage—and home of the top two scoring forwards in the league in the Edmonton Oilers.

You'll remember that we are already in rematch territory as the first matchup happened almost exactly a month ago in Edmonton. That game was a 6-5 shootout loss for the Canes where both teams surrendered five goals in regulation but the goalies managed to draw back in the shootout, with Brandon Bussi only surrendering the one that won the game.

Since that game Edmonton has been chugging right along, using the games in hand they built up with Carolina's Finland trip to overtake the Canes and stand second overall in the league on points—with Minnesota standing at the top this second. Carolina, though, leads on points percentage, so depending on your metric this is either a battle of two versus three or one versus three. Either way for a game this close to American Thanksgiving, it's got some heft.

The Oilers are finishing a six game road trip that hasn't really given them any breaks, as this game is actually the first of a road back-to-back for them with game two happening in St. Louis. The road trip started with a back-to-back as well, and in between they played the two Florida teams. They, like Carolina, are coming off a loss Thursday but unlike Carolina it was in regulation to the Atlantic leaders Tampa Bay—a team Carolina will see Wednesday.

You could do a deep dive on the Oilers in terms of trying to get to the bones of what makes them tick, but really you'd be trying too hard. The only stat you need to see is the top point producers in the league and you'll not only find three Oilers in the top four, you'll find Connor McDavid just blasting the competition with 55 points. He's doing it with—get ready—42 assists. He's "only" got thirteen goals, but the way he distributes the puck out on the ice has made him arguably an even bigger threat than he normally would be. He's well ahead of the pace he set last year of 90 assists, but actually behind the goal scoring pace which would frighten the league.

As for someone who's ahead of his goalscoring pace from last year, let's remind you of his teammate and usual linemate Leon Draisaitl. He's leading the league in goals with 24 right now, and he's basically pacing his career high in 2021-22 where he scored 55. Most teams would be lucky to have either, and you know that as much as Rod Brind'Amour loves this team he'd also be thrilled to have at least one.

So for all the controversy and jokes that came with the Mike Babcock hire, right now if you're Edmonton you're feeling pretty good about where things stand. They are already six points ahead of Vegas for first in the division, and fourteen ahead of Anaheim for third. Even the goaltending has been steadier than expected with both Devon Levi and Tristan Jarry holding sub-2.50 GAAs and over .900 save percentages. It's taken stress off the idea of getting Frederik Andersen back to the crease, as it looks like he won't be playing until Christmas. That means it is extremely unlikely he'll be in town for what would have been an emotional welcome back video, which is a shame as this is Edmonton's only regular season visit to Raleigh. Look for him to be acknowledged in some way though, and at least he got to spend time with Mike Maniscalco and Tripp Tracy back in October when the Canes visited Edmonton.

Speaking of that last visit, Babcock apparently is choosing the balanced lines approach, sliding Draisaitl to center his own line with Vasily Podkolzin (12 G, 8 A, +17) and Mathieu Jospeh (3 G, 5 A, -4). That creates the other line of McDavid, Filip Hallander (0G, 0A, -2) and Kasperi Kapanen (6 G, 7 A, -5). You may notice no Ryan Nugent-Hopkins on either line, and that's because he's once again dealing with injuries. He wasn't on the ice for Friday's practice, and while Babcock left it open that he would play Saturday, signs are pointing to no. Meanwhile, it appears Carolina will be facing Jarry based on how Edmonton practiced on Friday, which would be a switch up since it was Levi who backstopped the Oilers last month.

So why would Babcock opt for the balanced line appraoch? Well, last game the four Centers for Carolina were Sebastian Aho, Logan Stankoven, Jesperi Kotkaniemi, and Skyler Brind'Amour. Now you'll take out Kotkaniemi and Brind'Amour, and put in Jordan Staal and Mark Jankowski. That significantly upgrades the defensive ability of the team, thus spreading the goal scoring out appears to be how Babcock is answering.

For the Canes one would think the lack of a Friday practice means that Rod Brind'Amour will leave things alone, but in his mind, he could also feel that moving Seth Jarvis back up to the Aho line and William Carrier back to the Staal line wouldn't need any practice as both are very familiar with those roles. It's also hard to argue about the production the four lines as constructed are having right now, and Jarvis has proven over and over that he can defend with the Staal line. You also wonder where Kotkaniemi and Jussi Valimaki figure into the lineup over the next two days, as the back-to-back could be a way to get these two productive players back onto the ice and giving two others a load management day.

The question is which goalie gets the nod, understanding that the other will play in Madison Square Garden tomorrow. Logic would seem to dictate that with the tighter turnaround the Canes will go with Bussi today and Pyotr Kochetkov in New York, but Brind'Amour may flip that around to see how the Russian does with a tight turnaround and acknowledge he wasn't the reason the Canes lost Thursday. We don't know for sure because today was just an optional skate for the team, and only about half of the rostered players took part but with both goalies.

One other thing to note—last Thursday Taylor Hall played in his 1,000th NHL game being the first to do so playing in a European game. As is custom, the Hurricanes have a celebration planned that will be held Saturday before faceoff. If it follows the celebration for Frederik Andersen—hey maybe that's where he makes a surprise appearance—there'll be a montage, a few gifts including a silver hockey stick, and a surprise or two. So if you're planning to go account for that ceremony, and if you're watching on TV be ready.

All of that PLUS the second appearance of the Hometown Remix jerseys mean the start of an absolutely loaded weekend for the Hurricanes. If you aren't going to be there in person, here's how you can catch the action:

TV: Hurricanes Hockey Network and NHL Network. Hanna Yates and Shane Willis will handle the pregame duties at 12:30, and then Mike Maniscalco handles play-by-play with Tripp Tracy providing color—You can catch HHN on Spectrum, DirectTV, and Fubo. Note this game will also be nationally broadcast on NHL Network for fans outside of the Hurricanes' home market.

Streaming Video: Hurricanes Hockey Network on Prime Video in the Hurricanes Footprint. Outside the footprint with the game on NHL Network, you will NOT be able to watch the game on ESPN+.

Radio: The pregame Storm Watch with Adam Gold on 99.9 The Fan starts at 12:30 PM, At 1 PM radio (consisting of 99.9, 730 The Game in Charlotte, ESPN New Bern 107.5/1490, and ESPN Greenville 107.5/1570) will pick up the Mike Maniscalco and Tripp Tracy TV simulcast.

Line: (Per DraftKings) Hurricanes -180, -1.5 (+136) /Kraken +150, +1.5 (-162). O/U 6.5 +110/-130

#2875. Steelers-Eagles preview: What to expect from Philadelphia’s offense, defense Week 11

The Steelers will travel to Philadelphia — a city where they haven't won since 1965 — on Sunday in a game that many fans have had circled as an Eagles victory since before the season.

However, the 2026 Eagles aren't the same team that made the playoffs every year from 2021-25. They enter Week 11 with a dismal 2-7 record, with all those losses coming in a row against the Bears, Rams, Jaguars, Panthers, Cowboys, Commanders and Giants. Philadelphia hasn't been in the win column since a Sept. 20 victory over the Titans.

Is this Eagles team finally bad enough for the Steelers to break such a lengthy losing streak in The City of Brotherly Love? Or will Philadelphia, fresh off a Week 10 bye, be able to hold off the Steelers at home for yet another season? We'll find out in Week 11.

RB: It's been a rough year for the Eagles offense, now on its third quarterback of the season ahead of Week 11's matchup versus the Steelers. The numbers above speak for themselves: Philadelphia has struggled on both the ground and in the air in 2026, dealing with injuries, the sudden retirement of right tackle Lane Johnson, and poor execution from players on the field. The Eagles have only scored over 20 points four times this season, and have yet to break 30 once.

Before a Week 10 bye, the Eagles faced the New York Giants in Week 9, falling 19-11 in an ugly game that's been all too familiar for the Philly faithful. But one thing did change, with the Eagles trying to spark their Jalen Hurts-less offense by replacing backup Tanner McKee with Joe "Bazooka" Milton at quarterback. He's expected to start again against Pittsburgh on Sunday.

The results were not great against the Giants, with Milton finishing with 169 passing yards and 18/34 completion in Week 9. He didn't throw a single touchdown or interception, and was sacked a whopping seven times. However, the guy with the "Bazooka" nickname does indeed have a powerful arm, with Milton logging an 11.8% deep throw percentage along with eight air yards per attempt (both team highs for the Eagles this season). He's also a much better runner than McKee, leading the Eagles with 64 yards on the ground and a 7.1 average against New York.

Frankly, Milton plays like a still-developing quarterback. He holds onto the ball too long and doesn't seem to go through his reads at NFL speed. However, his arm talent does allow him to rifle balls into open zones when he sees them, and there's always a little bit of worry when playing a passer with his physical abilities — even if everything only clicks a few times a game, he can still make defenses pay. It's worth noting that Pittsburgh hasn't played many highly-mobile quarterbacks this year, and has had some issues with deep coverage this season.

With Milton under center, the Eagles have mixed up their playbook from McKee a bit with a few more rollouts and designed quarterback runs. What hasn't changed, however, is that Philadelphia remains an 11-personnel heavy team that likes short throws outside the numbers to move the ball in the passing game. Making matters worse is that despite the short passing game, Philadelphia ranks dead-last in the league in yards after catch in 2026, with just 738 on the year.

First-round receiver Makai Lemon is a name to watch as a twitchy underneath target, and Milton will get to throw to the now-healthy Devonta Smith for the first time this season after the latter missed the Giants game with injury. That's a big boost to what was a decimated Philly receiving core in Week 9. However, Dontayvion Wicks will still be out for the Eagles against Pittsburgh.

Capping the Eagles' offensive ceiling has been a rough year from the line, which lost All-World right tackle Lane Johnson to retirement in October and has struggled with some regression. Jordan Mailata remains a very good left tackle and Philadelphia now has Landon Dickerson back from injured reserve, but the Eagles still rank seventh-highest in sack rate allowed (9%), third-highest in pressure rate allowed (41.9%), and second-highest in runs stuffed percentage (23.5%) despite facing the seventh-most light boxes in the league.

Having inexperienced signal-callers since Hurts landed on injured reserve certainly hasn't helped the Eagles in pass protection. Philadelphia struggled mightily with interior pressure against the Giants, and gave up a number of unblocked rushers on stunts and twists.

One name to watch is rookie right tackle Markel Bell, a massive human being who has struggled some in pass protection this year, but is a promising run blocker.

Still, on the ground, the Eagles' inside zone-heavy attack is tied for 28th in the NFL in yards per rush (3.7), with just 28 non-Milton yards on the ground against New York. However, star running back Saquon Barkley, who played through injury against the Giants, should be rested up after the Eagles' bye and looking a bit more explosive. In two of Barkley's first three games this season, he averaged 5.5 yards per carry — when healthy, the 29-year-old is still a legitimate offensive weapon.

After a nightmare game against the Bengals last week, the Pittsburgh defense matches up much better against Philadelphia. The key will be stifling the Eagles' run game and pressuring Milton, which the Steelers' front seven is certainly capable of, especially with Derrick Harmon back in play on the interior. Milton averaged 3.12 seconds to throw last week — that should give TJ Watt more time to get home against a rookie right tackle.

Still, with Devonta Smith back in the lineup for the Eagles, along with solid ancillary weapons in Lemon and tight end Dallas Goedert available, a Pittsburgh secondary that got torched last week will still need to bring a better performance to the table. That said, there should be a lot more optimism heading into this week than the last for the Steelers' defense.

RP: Much like the Eagles' offense this year, the defensive side of the ball still possesses a lot of talent, but has performed well below expectations. A unit that was the driving force behind a Super Bowl victory just two seasons ago, the Eagles seemed poised to field a young and imposing defense that would keep them contenders for the rest of the 2020s. Instead, they were sellers at the deadline, shipping away defensive tackle Jordan Davis, whom they'd signed to a three-year, $78 million extension this March.

I spoke with Brandon Gowton of Bleeding Green Nation this week, to try to get a sense of why the defense has underperformed, and he said it's been one of the more baffling developments this season. He cited a lingering injury to superstar defensive tackle Jalen Carter and a general lack of pass rush as the biggest factor. Whether or not Carter is living up to the extension he signed this past summer (four years, $152 million) is seemingly a hot topic among Eagles fans and media, but a Steelers offensive line that just lost Mason McCormick should still be plenty concerned about his presence. Even in a "down" year, Carter's five sacks are only two off the NFL lead for defensive tackles.

As a defense, the Eagles' 16 sacks on the season rank 28th, and their 109 pressures rank 26th. According to Next Gen Stats, the Eagles are below league average in blitz rate (26% vs 32%), and in generating pressure, whether that's with the blitz (41% vs 43%) or without (30% vs 33%).

Carter's 20 pressures this season are tied for second on the Eagles behind only Moro Ojomo (22), an upcoming defensive tackle who allowed the Eagles the flexibility to trade away Davis. Jalyx Hunt, a rotational pass rusher who hasn't played more than 43% of the defensive snaps in a game since Week 4, is tied with Carter. Nolan Smith (17 pressures, 5 sacks), Jonathan Greenard (11 pressures, 1 sack), and Zack Baun (9 pressures, 2 sacks) round out the Eagles' underperforming pass rush.

Baun, the Eagles' top off-ball linebacker, is out for the game, the team announced Friday.

With the lack of pressure, the Eagles' secondary has predictably struggled. Third-year nickel corner Cooper DeJean has taken a step back, allowing four touchdowns this season after allowing just one during his first two seasons, and he has nearly as many missed tackles this season (13) as he had in his career (19) before this season. Meanwhile, Riq Woolen remains a volatile corner who has looked like a shutdown player some weeks and like a weak link in others, including surrendering four touchdowns against zero interceptions so far this season.

If there is one bright spot in Philadelphia's secondary, it's cornerback Quinyon Mitchell, who is still performing at a high level. He's allowed just a 55.6% catch rate and a QB passer rating of 76.5 when targeted. Philadelphia has typically let Mitchell stick to one side of the field, but in their most recent game, the Eagles finally tasked Mitchell with shadowing an opponent's number one receiver. Against the Giants, Mitchell followed Malik Nabers and shut down New York's most explosive playmaker.

As for what coverages we can expect from Philadelphia, the Eagles are a team that has lived in both nickel (68% of defensive snaps) packages and single-high safety looks (54% of snaps) for multiple seasons now. That has most typically shown up as Cover-3 looks in zone coverages (36.5%) and Cover-1 in man looks (15.3%).

On the ground, the Eagles aren't quite as bad as the numbers might indicate. While the Eagles' raw run defense totals look poor, they're mostly in the middle of the pack in most metrics, and are surrendering a high yardage per game total mostly because they have been in negative game scripts for much of the season. Philadelphia allows the seventh-fewest average yards before contact (0.81); however, they also allow the eighth-highest average yards after contact (3.33). As a unit, they've missed 43 tackles on running plays, per PFF, but only DeJean (8) and safety Andrew Mukuba (6) are particularly heinous in this regard. No other defender has more than three missed tackles against the run. Philadelphia ranks 15th in stuffed run percentage (17%), but are tied with the Vikings and Patriots for the 13th-most runs of 10+ yards allowed (27).

In theory, the Steelers' strength — running the ball — should fare respectably against this Eagles front. However, the loss of Mason McCormick cannot be overstated. Spencer Anderson takes his spot and has shown a troubling deficiency in play strength and power thus far in his career. He's excelled on the edges as a sixth offensive lineman, where he can take on lighter and less powerful defenders. Having him line up against Carter, though, gives me pause. Meanwhile, the Eagles' secondary should be gettable, but Rodgers has not played well this season. The Eagles haven't gotten pressure consistently, but Rodgers has often found his way into pressure even when the line is holding well. Now, without McCormick, there is potentially an easier path for the Philadelphia defensive line to get into the face of Pittsburgh's mobility-challenged passer.

This could be another frustrating day for the Black and Gold offense.

#2874. Hopewell football wins instant classic against Propel in WPIAL semifinals

DORMONT — When the game is on the line and you need a play to win the game, you're going to call on your best player.

That's exactly the situation that Hopewell found itself in the WPIAL 3A high school football semifinal game against Propel Braddock Hills. After a stop on defense, the team called on junior quarterback and Penn State commit James Armstrong, who took a keeper to the end zone to send the team to the WPIAL championship with a 66-60 double-overtime win on Friday, Nov. 20.

"In that situation, nobody is getting the ball other than No. 5 [James Armstrong]," Hopewell interim head coach John Rosa said. "Everyone played great tonight and when you have a Division I quarterback, you put the ball in his hands and let him win you the game."

The semifinal victory for Hopewell will go down as one of the best games in WPIAL playoff history, featuring 126 combined points between the two sides.

But it wasn't a back-and-forth affair throughout, with Hopewell (12-1) having to claw its way back from multiple deficits, including a 16-point hole halfway through the third quarter.

After Propel Braddock Hills (8-4) struck for an 11-yard touchdown run by running back Daniel Stephens, Armstrong threw an interception to Mikal Hall, setting up an 85-yard pick-six that — in many games — would have been the nail in the coffin.

Following the pick-six, Hopewell answered, with Brody Rock and Avery Bokor combining on a kickoff return, as Rock flipped the ball to Bokor at the Propel 12-yard-line and ran the rest of the way.

Related Reading: Hopewell football head coach Matt Mottes suspended for WPIAL 3A semifinals

Required Reading: WPIAL high school football Beaver County semifinal scoreboard

Required Reading: WPIAL high school football semifinal Beaver County storylines

After the return, Hopewell recovered an onside kick and, a few plays later, Armstrong ran for his third score of the night. The junior finished with six total touchdowns and an even 200 yards rushing.

"Not saying that tonight was going to be my best game ever, but I told the guys that I was going to try my hardest," Armstrong said. "When I threw that pick-six, I was thinking to myself that it was going to be difficult to come back from. But once I got to the sideline, I knew I just needed to keep working hard because I knew the defense would have my back."

That Hopewell defense came up big when it mattered most, despite what the box score said.

Just like he has all season, Propel athlete and Colorado commit Gabe Jenkins has terrorized opposing defenses and against Hopewell it was no different, finishing with 357 yards and five total touchdowns.

However, that Hopewell defense intercepted Jenkins twice, including once on the goal line with Propel threatening to score in the second quarter and also breaking up a pass in the end zone during the second overtime period.

"Our kids bent and had a lot of adversity throughout this game," Rosa said. "When this game started, we faced a lot of adversity, but all our coaches kept telling them that we had a lot of game left. They could have given up, but they didn't. This group kept working and I am so proud of them."

Hopewell will now shift its focus to the WPIAL Class 3A championship game, where it is making its first appearance since 2009.

The matchup will feature the much-anticipated Vikings and top-seeded Avonworth, a team that Hopewell beat back in Week 1.

"I've been going down to Acrisure since I was a kid to watch these WPIAL championship games but this year I have been saying on the sidelines that I'm not going to them this year, I am going to play in it," Armstrong said. "No matter what happens, if we just give our all, we will come out victorious."x

#2872. Saints officially waive S Justin Reid

Safety Justin Reid already penned his good-bye missive to the Saints, but his departure did not become official until 4 p.m. ET.

The Saints waived Reid on Friday, with the move made official on the NFL’s personnel notice.

Reid asked for his release this week, and the Saints have granted it.

“In life and sports, things don’t always go the way you planned or hoped they would,” Reid wrote on social media. “Unfortunately, this is one of those moments. But that doesn’t change the love and respect I have for this organization or city. I truly want to see this team and this city thrive. No one deserves it more than New Orleans and the Saints.”

He will go through waivers, with the NFL announcing at 4 p.m. ET on Monday whether he clears. If no team claims him, Reid will become a free agent.

Reid joined the Saints as a free agent in 2025, and he started all 24 games he played for the team. He had 118 tackles, an interception and a half-sack.

#2869. Giants shocked to learn Colts cut ex-teammate Daniel Jones

John Harbaugh's eyes nearly popped out of his head upon learning that the Colts cut quarterback Daniel Jones.

Harbaugh wasn't the Giants head coach when they stunningly cut Jones in November 2024, so they don't have the personal relationship that Jones maintains with others in East Rutherford. But the Colts are next week's opponent, so the Giants' behind-the-scene staffers in charge of advance scouting were just thrown a major curveball.

The Colts plan to start first-round draft bust Anthony Richardson, which means an offense that could be more option-based in store on Nov. 29 to hide Richardson's passing inaccuracy.

Daniel Jones has been released by the Colts. Getty Images

"[It changes] probably pretty dramatically. I mean, wow," Harbaugh admitted. "I don't want to get ahead of ourselves here, and you just hit me with that. But definitely the offense will be the offense that they run, but it'll be centered around the quarterback for sure, and they're probably different styles to some degree, so we'll have to look at that when the time comes. We have people doing that this week. They'll have to make a left turn, obviously."

Unlike when the Giants demoted Jones from starter to third-stringer to protect themselves against an injury clause in his contract, Jones did not ask for his release from the Colts, head coach Shane Steichen said.

In the Giants locker room, news of Jones' release brought back memories. What does it say to teammates when you release the quarterback who started the first 11 games?

"That the writing is on the wall for the season and you have to get through the next six games the best you can play," right tackle Jermaine Eluemunor told The Post. "D.J. is a great guy but he's in such a predicament. He went there last year and balled out so it's definitely not a 'him' thing. When you trade everyone like they did, it's hard."

John Harbaugh, head coach of the Giants. Getty Images

Giants backup offensive lineman Josh Sills was with the Colts last season and to start 2026. He saw the same hard-working Jones who was beloved by Giants teammates.

"He helped me in any and every way imaginable," Sills told The Post. "He went above and beyond to make sure everyone had what they needed to achieve their goals. It's a team game. It takes 11 guys."

Next week was billed as the Jones Revenge Game. Instead, Jones didn't even make it through the first year of the two-year, $88 million contract that he signed after a torn Achilles.

Anthony Richardson will now start at quarterback for the Colts. Getty Images

Jones might be able to help the Patriots or Ravens in their playoff chase. Or he could help the Eagles not be an embarrassment the rest of the season. Or use his ties to Broncos offensive coordinator Davis Webb to become the backup for a Super Bowl contender.

Any team that claims him off waivers will inherit $2.1 million in salary this season and a $10.5 million injury guarantee for 2027.

Returning to the Giants or reuniting with Brian Daboll in Tennessee are extremely unlikely possibilities.

"I wish the best for him and I know he's going to make the most of his next opportunity," left tackle Andrew Thomas said. "But I'm focused on what we have going on and we're not looking at Indy right now. We're focused on this week and Jacksonville."

#2868. NFL will consider requiring minimum-night stays for international games

After the 49ers and the Rams took dramatically different approaches to their arrival in Melbourne for the first-ever Australia game, we raised the question of whether the league should require teams to get there early enough to advance the goal of promoting American football to international audiences.

Via The Athletic, the NFL will consider adopting a minimum-night stay for teams playing internationally.

“Currently, we leave those football decisions on when the team arrives in the market to the club,” NFL executive V.P. of club business, major events, and international Peter O’Reilly told The Athletic. “It is something that we’ll consider moving forward on: Do we put some minimum nights in a market in there? It’s something we’ll look at.”

The league already does it for the Super Bowl, mandating a full-week experience that includes multiple press conferences, the annual “Opening Night” media extravaganza, and practicing at designated facilities.

“Having access to players and that type of experience when they’re in the market does have a lasting impact,” O’Reilly said. “That’s a factor when you start to think about encouraging or potentially looking at a policy where you’d want a club in the market for more than the day before.”

Beyond impacting strategic considerations, it would add to the total expense. The money would be worth it, if it further enhances the league’s overall reason for going global.

#2866. Amari Jones and Gabriela Fundora Give Fans the Goods Friday

Undefeated middleweight Amari Jones says he brought out the dog Friday night in the Golden Boy Boxing main event Friday in Indio, California.

Undisputed women's flyweight champion Gabriela Fundora scored two knockdowns but settled for a dominating decision over challenger Jasmina Zapotoczna of England.

Coachella Valley-based boxers sent their fans home happy with satisfying knockout wins.

DeAundre Pettus had avoided being stopped as a professional boxer, but his luck ran out when "The Reaper" Amari Jones came for him on Friday night at the Fantasy Springs Resort and Casino.

Jones of Oakland (18-0, 16 KOs) got it done in eight rounds against a game Pettus of South Carolina (14-5, 7 KOs).

Jones is currently the IBF mandatory challenger to champion Aaron McKenna of Ireland. McKenna's mandatory isn't due anytime soon, so Jones didn't want to sit around. He said he was glad to face an opponent who could give him some good rounds in Pettus.

"I've been waiting for a guy like that to give me some rounds. He was athletic, a little crafty, a little less athletic than I thought. He put together fast, twitchy movements and that's always tricky no matter how experienced a guy is. It just didn't work," said Jones.

Jones was patient, knowing his power punches were more relentless and skilled than anything Pettus had seen before. He had earned his reputation as a tough fighter, and he made Jones earn the win

The end came from a check left hook a minute into the eighth round. Pettus went down hard. Referee Thomas Taylor gave him a hard look, and decided the night was over. Pettus protested but it was the right decision.

A vicious check left hook was the end for DeAundre Pettus. Photo: Golden Boy Boxing

"I showed a little bit more dog. You all know I come forward and get a guy a little more technical. Today I dug a little more deeper and dug it out more than I normally do," said Jones.

Jones revealed that he had worked as a sparring partner for Canelo Álvarez in preparation for his fight with Christian Mbilli, and picked up a few moves, including the technique Álvarez uses to batter his opponents to the arms. "I was definitely going to use it in my next fight."

Jones promised The Reaper is here to stay, and sent a message out to the world champion."Aaron McKenna, where you at, Leprechaun Man? I'm coming for the pot of gold. You've got it, and I'm going to get it."

Gabriela Fundora came up just short of stopping Jasmina Zapotoczna on Friday, but there was more than enough action to send fans home happy. Photo: Golden Boy Boxing

It was the co-main event, but everyone in Indio considered their hometown champion Gabriela "Sweet Poison" Fundora (19-0, 10 KOs) the star of the show. The undisputed WBA, WBC, WBO and IBF flyweight world champion and The Ring magazine champion didn't disappoint them.

While Fundora couldn't deliver a knockout, she scored two knockdowns and gave fans ten rounds of action against former European flyweight champion Jasmina Zapotoczna of England (10-2). Scores were 100-88, 99-91, and 98-92.

Fundora kept up a furious pace from start to finish. If she had three-minute rounds to work with, she would have followed up her knockdowns with enough time to make them stick. But she wasn't too disappointed.

"She's a mover. She had a game plan, and her first time was not too shoddy … I was just trying to get the knockout in. But my father said, 'Hey, you can't knock everybody out.' So, it's fine," said Fundora.

Fundora kept Zapotoczna in front of her, sizing her up. Fundora suffered an accidental headbutt in the first few seconds of the fight. It likely made the southpaw fighter more cautious about making things worse.

But it wasn't long before the uppercuts, body shots, and hooks landed. The fans cheered every thudding punch. Fundora said she hit the openings, but with Zapotoczna on the move, it was a challenge. "She was running around the ring, so she was trying to survive. That's cool, but hey, this is boxing."

Gabriela Fundora punishes her opponents and fans love her for it. Photo: Golden Boy Boxing

Fundora scored knockdowns in both the seventh and eighth rounds on combinations of body shots and hooks. It was surprising that Zapotczna's corner allowed her to come out for the ninth round, but she finished the fight on her feet.

"The bell won this time. But it was an exciting fight. I got to showcase a couple different things, and it was just Sweet Poison time."

Asked about her future, Fundora didn't name anyone. Instead, she made the case that she is the fighter fans come to see and the opponent hardly matters.

"Whoever steps into the ring, they're coming to see Fundora, just like Michael Jackson, Prince, Oscar De La Hoya. They want to see both people. So. I want to be one of those names that they're just seeing Fundora. Doesn't matter who's in the ring." It's true in the Coachella Valley without a doubt.

Joel Iriarte had his opponent Darvin Galeano hurt and using the ropes to hold him up. He didn't last much longer. Photo: Golden Boy Boxing

Joel Iriarte of Bakersfield, California (12-0, 11 KOs) was a man on a mission against Darvin Galeano of Panama (15-5, 9 KOs). He overwhelmed Galeano with his offense. By the second round, Galeano was losing the will to continue, his legs failing under him.

As Iriarte pinned him against the ropes, Galeano sat on them, letting them hold him up. The sharp-eyed referee, Michael Margardo, properly called it a knockdown, and after giving the count, Galeano decided his night was over at 2:31 of Round 2.

"The first jab, literally, we see him broken in his spirit," said Iriarte. He said he'd like to return to the ring quickly on January 23 as part of the Arnold Barboza Jr. vs Oscar Duarte undercard in Anaheim.

Cayden Griffiths says his bread and butter is his body work, and he's right. Photo: Golden Boy Boxing

Cayden Griffiths of Indio (9-0, 8 KOs) added a knockout win to his resume at super lightweight against Jean Rivera Pacheco of Puerto Rico (10-3, 1 KO). Rivera decided early to stay on the move after taking a few shots. Griffiths had to follow Rivera and later said he was frustrated by it. He got to Rivera in round six with a perfect left hook to the liver, and the fight was over at 1:59 of round six.

"I know my body shots are my bread and butter. I knew once I landed clean, he wasn't going to be able to take it," said Griffiths, but he was hard on himself for not being able to pin Rivera down more quickly, and said he's got some things to work on in the gym. "The end result is all that really matters, and we got him out of there." Griffiths is only 20 years old, so he's got time to work on his craft.

Ruslan Abdullaev is ready for tougher opposition. Photo: Golden Boy Boxing

Undefeated Uzbek Olympian Ruslan Abdullaev (6-0, 4 KOs) was never threatened by Juan Meza Angulo of Mexico (9-4, 6 KOs), but credit Mesa for hanging in tough while taking a hiding, peppered with snapping jabs, followed by relentless body punching and hooks.

Abdullaev's punishment finally took its toll as referee Ray Corona waived off the fight after Meza went down from a 1-2 combination of hooks at 2:40 of round six. Abdullaev picks up the WBA Continental Gold super lightweight belt.

Middleweight Amir "The Cashman" Anderson of Syracuse, New York (9-0, 7 KOs) drilled Daniel Buciuc of Romania (16-4, 6 KOs) for two hard rounds, scoring a knockdown at the end of two. We could say Buciuc was saved by the bell, but not really, as Anderson came straight at him and dropped him again with a vicious left hook to the body for a third-round knockout at 23 seconds of the round.

Lightweight Leonardo "Bazooka" Sanchez of Cathedral City (12-0, 10 KOs) had an impressive number of local supporters in the seats. They only got to see him for a round, but he packed plenty of action into those few minutes and lived up to his moniker. He went right at Victor "El Tornado" Morales of Vancouver, BC (20-2-1, 10 KOs), first with body shots for his first knockdown, right and left hooks for the second, ending the fight at 2:48 of the round.

Making his Golden Boy debut, Sebastian "Lil Beast" Mitry of Phoenix (8-0, 6 KOs) put in six solid rounds against Anthony Alvarado of Salem, Oregon (9-2, 2 KOs) in his middleweight bout. Scores were 60-54. 60-54, and 59-55. Mitry is based at the Diaz Brothers gym in Indio where he's been impressing the trainers as a sparring partner. He fought his first seven fights in Mexico.

Undefeated super lightweight Javier Meza of Amarillo, Texas (7-0, 4 KOs) scored his first knockdown over Jonathan Figueroa of Hartford, Connecticut (8-3, 7 KOs) in the third round, then made it stick with a body-shot knockout in the fourth.

Opening the night, welterweight David Lopez of Las Vegas (10-0, 7 KOs) made it quick with a first-round knockout over Alan Crisosto of Guadalajara (12-8, 8 KOs).

#2864. Pistons vs Raptors Discussion: Game Time, TV, Odds, and More

The Toronto Raptors are scuffling in the first leg of the NBA season, but there is certainly a lot of talent there. Yes, Kawhi Leonard is absent with news of managing knee pain and swelling that surely puts a shiver up the spine of every Toronto fan. But they also still have Scottie Barnes, and he is delivering the young star goods as advertised. He is averaging 23.8 points, 7.4 rebounds, and 4.7 assists a night. On certain nights, he looks like the young superstar that represents his clearly attainable ceiling. On other nights, he and his team's offense struggle and can't seem to get unstuck from the mud. He's doing a lot of things, but he isn't doing it as efficiently or consistently as you'd like.

The real problems with the Raptors, though, lie elsewhere. Nobody can seemingly hit a three. RJ Barrett is getting to the line five times a game and hitting just 60% from the charity stripe. Collin Murray-Boyles has regressed, and Jakob Poeltl's game has fallen off a cliff. It feels like if you solve Scottie, it helps you solve Barrett and Immanuel Quickley, and therefore, you solve the Raptors. Toronto has dropped six straight, and will be without the services of Barrett tonight.

Watching an Ausar Thompson and Scottie Barnes matchup could be electric. Barnes doesn't have the defensive chops Ausar does, and Ausar doesn't have the offensive versatility, but both could be liable to pull off a triple-double tonight. Cade Cunningham is also expected back tonight after sitting out Detroit's win against the New Orleans Pelicans. Duncan Robinson and Ron Holland are out, and doubtful, respectively.
