#2822. MLB rumors: Phillies OF on the trading block, Blue Jays eye ex-Yankee, plus Mets, Mariners, Braves, Rays, more

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Thursday marks the final day of the General Manager meetings in Las Vegas. Though we haven't seen any major moves this offseason, we have seen an uptick in rumors in the past few days and that isn't a coincidence. Put a bunch of reporters and front-office execs in the same area for three or four days and chatter picks up, even if there isn't a ton of actual substance behind it.

Rumors are always fun, so let's round up the latest right here in addition to any news.

We have one of our first major-league deals of the offseason as the Braves have signed reliever Evan Phillips to a three-year, $35 million deal. Phillips ranked No. 34 on our top 50 free agent list.

Phillips, a 32-year-old right-hander, has been a setup man or closer for the Dodgers for the last several seasons. In returning from surgery last season, he appeared in 31 games, pitching to a 3.46 ERA and 1.07 WHIP with 33 strikeouts in 26 innings. The majority of his closing experience came in 2023 when he had 24 saves in 27 chances.

The Braves' closer last season was Raisel Iglesias, but he's a free agent. Holdover Robert Suarez could take over those duties while Phillips, lefty Dylan Lee and youngster Didier Fuentes are also in the late-inning mix.

The Blue Jays went from one of the best offensive teams in 2025 to one of the worst in 2026. They'd love a big bounceback from Vladimir Guerrero Jr., among other positive movements, but they also look for outside help. Might that help come in the form of free agent infielder Jazz Chisholm Jr.?

The Jays have met with representatives for Chisholm, reports Sportsnet.

Chisholm, 28, hit .231/.312/.399 last season, but had a huge 2025 and any signing team would be hoping for the 2025 version of Chisholm.

The Jays do have a capable incumbent second baseman in Ernie Clement, but either Clement or Chisholm can be used in the outfield and the Blue Jays certainly need outfield upgrades.

The Phillies have been telling opposing teams that they are not "locked into anything" regarding their current outfield situation, according to The Athletic, so much so that All-Star outfielder Brandon Marsh is available in a trade.

As things stand, with Bryce Harper moving back to first base, the Phillies would have Bryan De La Cruz in right, Justin Crawford in center and Marsh in left, but that all seems pretty fluid as they look to upgrade the ballclub and keep that window of contention open.

Marsh, 28, had a stellar start last season, leading to his first-ever All-Star Game. Through July 1, he hit .319/.352/.530. The rest of the way, however, he slashed .177/.241/.241, dragging him down overall to a 90 OPS+ and 0.4 WAR on the season.

The Mariners have shown interest in free agent left fielder Ian Happ, per MLB Network.

Happ, 32, has spent his entire 10-year MLB career with the Cubs, who drafted him in the first round all the way back in 2015. He was pretty consistent from 2022-25, averaging 3.9 WAR with a .251/.347/.433 (118 OPS+) line and winning four Gold Gloves. Last season, though, he dipped to .218/.326/.412 (103 OPS+) with 2.7 WAR. He still topped 20 home runs for the fifth time in the last six years.

The Mariners have a vacancy in left field with Randy Arozarena having hit free agency and a need to upgrade their offense.

Yesterday's big news was the Yankees' interest in free agent first/third baseman Alec Bohm and The Athletic reports that we can now add the Mets to the list of suitors.

Bohm hit .249/.315/.383 (87 OPS+) with 15 home runs, 88 RBI and -0.2 WAR last season. It might be surprising to see him garnering such interest on the open market, but there isn't much right-handed power out there and Bohm finished last season strong (.318/.402/.453 in his last 42 games). The Mets, meanwhile, are desperate for help at the cold corner after last year's disastrous Jorge Polanco experiment. Jared Young did provide a glimmer of hope at first base before suffering a concussion in September while colliding with a teammate on a play.

Free agent starting pitcher Dustin May has a decent market right now, according to MLB Network, with at least the Cubs, White Sox, Padres and Astros having shown interest in the right-hander.

May, 29, made 31 starts between the Cardinals and Brewers last season, going 7-9 with a 4.70 ERA in 145 ⅔ innings. He showed flashes of brilliance with the Cardinals and had a stretch from April 10 to June 15 during which he posted a 2.54 ERA in 12 starts, but in the end there was plenty of inconsistency and even bad, forcing him into a bullpen role in the playoffs for the champion Brewers.

Free agent catcher Ryan Jeffers has been extended the qualifying offer from the Twins, meaning he has to decide by net Tuesday whether to accept the one-year, $23.15 million contract or hit free agency. There's nothing that says a team can't re-sign a player after he declines their qualifying offer, of course, and it sounds like that path is possible in this case.

Jeffers will decline the QO and the Twins are interested in reaching a multi-year deal to bring him back, reports the Minnesota Star-Tribune. Jeffers hit .256/.353/.461 with 14 home runs for Minnesota in 2026, a standout line for a catcher, though he missed several months with a broken hamate. The Twins do have several catchers in their farm system, including Vahn Lackey, the No. 3 pick in last year's draft, and Eduardo Tait, who headlined the trade return for Jhoan Duran.

Much has been said and will continue to be said about the 2027 free agent class and how it looks relatively weak, especially on the position-player side, where the best players are the likes of Jazz Chisholm Jr., Seiya Suzuki, and the aforementioned Arozarena and Jeffers.

As we know, the Rays love to find avenues where they can exploit market inefficiencies. Their latest move might be to hit the trade market with center fielder Jonny DeLuca in anticipation of moving Victor Mesa Jr. to center, according to The Athletic.

DeLuca, 28, got more playing time than ever before in 2026, appearing in 112 games and getting 370 plate appearances. He hit .266/.300/.429 (101 OPS+) with 11 homers, 50 RBI, 17 steals and 1.7 WAR.

Fresh off a playoff berth despite the lowest payroll in all of baseball at a paltry $75ish million, the Guardians are planning to increase payroll. Or so they say.

"We'll go up," club president Chris Antonetti told The Athletic. "We have to."

The Guardians hosted six playoff games and made some money there, not to mention that they have a bunch of players hitting arbitration and surely they want to spend more to remain competitive. There's an added wrinkle here, too, which would be that the ownership side is really pushing hard for a salary cap when the upcoming CBA expires in a few weeks. With a cap comes a floor and we've seen MLB float proposals with salary floors in the $180 million range, or well more than double what the Guardians had last season.

So, yeah, I'd say the Guardians "have to" increase payroll, one way or another.

#2821. Astros slugger Yordan Alvarez named unanimous American League MVP after nearly winning 2026 Triple Crown

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Astros designated hitter Yordan Alvarez has won the 2026 American League MVP, the BBWAA announced Thursday night.

The Baseball Writers' Association of America ballot uses two voters from each of the 15 chapters housing AL teams. Each voter submits a ballot with 10 players, ranked 1-10. A first-place vote gets a player 14 points, while second place earns nine, third place eight, etc., until a 10th-place vote earns one point. Ballots are due before the playoffs begin, as this is a regular-season award.

Alvarez, 29, bounced back from an injury-riddled disaster in 2025, during which he only played in 48 games. This time around, he avoided the IL and appeared in 158 games. He hit .316/.432/.601 (190 OPS+) with 34 doubles, a triple, 42 home runs, 106 RBI, 105 runs and 6.8 WAR. He made a late run at the Triple Crown, missing out by one home run and seven RBI. He did lead the majors in all three triple slash categories, though, leading in average by five points, on-base percentage by a whopping 42 points -- he led the majors in walks -- and 31 points in slugging percentage. Add the latter two and his OPS victory was 90 points -- 1.033 to second place's .943 (National League MVP Pete Crow-Armstrong).

The only players in MLB history to lead the majors in all three triple slash categories:

Alvarez becomes just the third Astros player to win MVP after Jeff Bagwell in 1994 and Jose Altuve in 2017.

Rays third baseman Junior Caminero finished in second. At age 22, he played all 162 games, leading the Rays to the best AL record. He hit .276/.360/.524 (144 OPS+) with 42 home runs, 101 RBI and 100 runs, posting a 5.7 WAR. He's already made history, too, as the Rays had previously never had a player finish in the top five of MVP voting.

Royals shortstop Bobby Witt Jr. came in third. This is now his fourth straight season in the top seven and third straight season in the top four. He finished second in 2024. This season, he hit .281/.359/.441 (125 OPS+) with 33 doubles, 18 homers, 54 RBI, 94 runs, 45 steals and 6.7 WAR.

Brooklyn's Moritz Wagner has been in the NBA for nine years and alive for 20 more, and he had never, ever played against his brother, Franz, in a game that counted.

That is, until Friday night. The Wagner siblings faced off when the Nets hosted Franz's Orlando Magic, and they squared off against each other for the first time.

"It's awkward, I'm not gonna lie. I don't really know how to compartmentalize it, really deal with it, and try to focus on the game plan and see what happens emotionally," Moritz said beforehand. "It's a dilemma because you want him to do well. At the same time, you game plan for him, you prepare to limit him. So, it's an awkward feeling. I've never been in this position.

Brooklyn Nets center Moritz Wagner (13) warms up before an NBA basketball game against the Orlando Magic at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, NY, Nov. 20, 2026. Robert Sabo for NY Post

"It's weird. It's definitely a new role I've got to grow into. But it's also fun, and we talked about that [Thursday]. It's an absolute privilege, and for our family it's really cool because we've established each other as independent NBA players on two different sides and get to play against each other in this league and have 20 people here watching us. So it's very special, and it's gonna be an emotional night for our family."

Moritz played for Michigan and was a 2018 first-round pick by Orlando, where he'd spent his entire career until signing with the Nets this summer.

Franz, four years his junior, had followed in his footsteps and starred for the Wolverines before being picked eighth overall by the Magic in 2021.

Orlando Magic's Franz Wagner handles the ball during an NBA Cup game against the Brooklyn Nets at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, N.Y., Friday, Nov. 20, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

"My whole NBA career I played with him. The first time in my life playing against him," Franz said. "But as weird as it's going to be, it's a blessing to share the court with your family.Not a lot of people get to do that, so that's how I look at it."

The younger Wagner is breaking out this season with Paolo Banchero injured, entering the meeting averaging 24.4 points, 8.5 assists and 6.9 rebounds.

And it was 30.7 points, 8.5 assists and 7.7 boards over his prior six games.

"We've got a new staff, a new system and a lot of clarity on roles and what's expected of everyone. That helps everyone with their rhythm," Franz said.

"He's playing incredible," Moritz said. "New coach, new system, found a way to be a playmaker and scorer at the same time, dominating the pace, the paint. Obviously with an enormous usage rate as well, which you've got to give him credit for the physicality he plays with. One thing that's really underrated about Franz is his defensive ability. There aren't many players that dominate on both ends of the floor like he does. … It's a joy to watch him as a big brother, regardless of my situation."

Young fan Dylan wears a custom jersey featuring Orlando Magic forward Franz Wagner and Brooklyn Nets center Moritz Wagner in Brooklyn, N.Y., Nov. 20, 2026. Robert Sabo for NY Post

That situation is serving as backup center and veteran voice on a struggling Nets squad.

Many have suggested that Brooklyn could target Franz if Orlando breaks him and Banchero up. But for the moment, the Nets have to figure out their own woes internally, and Franz was the enemy Friday.

"I grew up with him. When he's mad, when I see that look in his eyes, it takes me back to when we were 9 and 5 and playing against each other. I don't think I know a human better than him. So on an emotional level, just knowing what he goes through with every step, it's weird to be the opponent," Moritz said.

"Obviously, [he's] a big part of my development as a person and as a player," Franz said. "So it should be fun out there."

Orlando Magic forward Franz Wagner (22) warms up before a game against the Brooklyn Nets on Friday, Nov. 20, 2026, at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, N.Y. Robert Sabo for NY Post

The Wagner family was on hand to watch Friday. They even had specialized jerseys made with both of the brothers' names and team colors, and their mother Beate is especially nervous.

"We made these corny split jerseys," Moritz said. "I know my mom's going to go through it [Friday]. I know that. … That's going to be really difficult for her. Definitely a new spot for her. … But she's going to figure that out, and it's also a really good problem to have, obviously.."

#2819. Jason Crowe Jr. and Bryson Tiller score 18 apiece for Missouri in 91-64 win over Le Moyne

COLUMBIA, Mo. (AP) — Jason Crowe Jr. and Bryson Tiller scored 18 points apiece to lead five Missouri players in double figures and the Tigers beat Le Moyne 91-64 on Friday night for their fourth consecutive win.

Kennard Davis Jr. had 13 points, Jamier Jones scored 11 and Toni Bryant 10 for Missouri (5-1).

Khalil Williams, Ty Francis and Trevor Rowe scored 11 points apiece for Le Moyne (2-4).

The Tigers shot 57% from the field, 11 of 25 (55%) from 3-point range, scored 26 points off 16 Le Moyne turnovers and outscored the Dolphins 12-3 in second-chance points.

Crowe hit a jumper that gave Missouri the lead for good less than 5 minutes into the game. Crowe, Pierce and Tiller each hit a 3 in an 85-second span to push the lead into double figures and spark a 17-4 run that culminated with back-to-back dunks by Crowe and Jones and made it 31-15 with 8:26 left until halftime.

Tiller hit a jumper and a 3-pointer that ignited a 12-0 to close the first half. Bryant added a layup and, after Cord Stansberry took a steal coast to coast for a two-hand dunk, made a 3 that gave the Tigers an 28-point lead at halftime.

Jones made two free throws before Crowe stole the inbounds pass, calmly took one dribble to the right corner and knocked down a 3 and Davis followed with a steal and a fast-break dunk 19 seconds later that gave Missouri its biggest lead of the game at 63-26 with 15 minutes to go.

Le Moyne: Hosts Division-II Mansfield on Monday.

#2818. Cubs superstar Pete Crow-Armstrong wins National League MVP unanimously, beating Shohei Ohtani

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Cubs center fielder Pete Crow-Armstrong won the National League MVP award for the 2026 season unanimously, the BBWAA revealed Thursday evening.

The Baseball Writers' Association of America ballot uses two voters from each of the 15 chapters housing NL teams. Each voter submits a ballot with 10 players, ranked 1-10. A first-place vote gets a player 14 points, while second place earns nine, third place eight, etc., until a 10th-place vote earns one point. Ballots are due before the playoffs begin, as this is a regular-season award.

Though the results were unsurprising by the time the year wound down, going back to the start of the season, this marks a huge upset. Shohei Ohtani was the overwhelming favorite to win the award heading into the season and maintained favorite status through the All-Star break. A combination of his injuries and PCA's monster season turned the tide late.

Appearing in all 162 games, Crow-Armstrong hit .280/.372/.570 (156 OPS+) with 31 doubles, eight triples, 45 home runs, 107 RBI, 119 runs, 41 steals and 9.8 WAR. He led the majors in WAR, runs, home runs and total bases while leading the NL in slugging, OPS and OPS+. He became the seventh player in MLB history to join the 40-40 club (40 home runs and 40 steals in the same season). Further, he led the majors in multiple advanced defensive metrics, putting together one of the great all-around seasons from a position player in recent memory.

PCA joins the following list of Cubs to win MVP:

Ohtani, the four-time MVP, finishes in second. At the plate, Ohtani hit .275/.377/.512 with 26 doubles, four triples, 30 home runs, 81 RBI, 89 runs, 11 steals and 3.2 WAR. On the mound, he was 8-2 with a 1.79 ERA, 0.95 WHIP and 95 strikeouts in 85 ⅔ innings with 3.2 WAR.

The nationally underrated Brewers second baseman Brice Turang finishes in third. He still hasn't made an All-Star Game, though he did start 2026 as a member of Team USA in the World Baseball Classic. In the regular season, Turang hit .268/.366/.450 (124 OPS+) with 37 doubles, three triples, 21 home runs, 100 RBI, 102 runs and 7.3 WAR, rating out as one of the best defenders in baseball.

#2815. Lakers Player Ratings: Luka Scores 25 Points To Barely Come Away With Victory Over Kings

The Los Angeles Lakers survived a serious scare against the Sacramento Kings, escaping with a narrow 106-104 victory despite struggling to control the glass and protect their lead.

Luka Doncic led the way with 25 points and eight assists, while Walker Kessler and Matisse Thybulle were solid on both ends of the floor. However, this was far from a convincing performance from Los Angeles, which allowed Sacramento to collect 44 rebounds and stay within striking distance.

The Lakers shot an efficient 50.0% from the field, compared to Sacramento's 45.6%, but their 30.6% shooting from three-point range left plenty to be desired.

Los Angeles also had to overcome a 16-9 disadvantage in offensive rebounds, giving the Kings second chances throughout the night. The difference came at the free-throw line, where the Lakers converted 17 of 22 attempts compared to Sacramento's nine of 12.

Doncic had the biggest scoring night, but this victory was more of a collective survival effort than a dominant showing from the superstar. Here are the player ratings for every Laker who saw the floor.

Game Stats: 25 PTS, 4 REB, 8 AST, 2 STL, 6 TOV, 9-18 FG, 2-9 3-PT FG, 5-6 FT, 36 MIN

Still, Doncic delivered when the Lakers needed offensive production. His eight assists helped keep the offense moving, and his two steals added some defensive value.

The 25-point performance was enough to lead Los Angeles to victory, even if the overall efficiency and ball security left room for improvement.

He also recorded a steal and a block without committing a single turnover. Thybulle's five-point plus/minus was another positive sign. His ability to contribute on both ends made him one of the most efficient players in the Lakers' lineup.

#2814. Now with an MVP, Pete Crow-Armstrong is on the path for an all-time great career. Where could he stack up?

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The MVP winners were revealed Thursday evening and, unsurprisingly, the runaway unanimous winner in the National League was Pete Crow-Armstrong of the Cubs.

Crow-Armstrong had an all-time great regular season, which is what the annual Baseball Writers' Association of America award honors. He hit .280/.372/.570 with stellar defense, led the league in home runs (tied with Kyle Schwarber) and massively upgraded his on-base skills. Now, just a few months shy of his 25th birthday, he has an MVP to his name.

So what's next? To answer that, we'll look backwards.

Since integration, only eight center fielders have posted at least 8.0 WAR in a season age-25 or younger: Willie Mays, Mickey Mantle, Willie Davis, César Cedeño, Ken Griffey Jr., Andruw Jones, Mike Trout and PCA. The only ones to top PCA's 9.8 were Mays, Mantle and Trout. If we went back to pre-integration, Tris Speaker and Ty Cobb were the only others who bested PCA here.

Willie Davis was an inconsistent hitter through his age-23 season but had flashed a nice power-speed combo. Sound familiar? That gaudy 8.4 WAR total in 1964, though, was driven largely by monster defensive metrics. Davis hit .294/.316/.413 (110 OPS+) with 23 doubles, seven triples, 12 home runs, 77 RBI, 91 runs and 42 steals. There's little doubt that PCA's 2026 season dwarfed this one overall, and Davis was far behind Mays (11.0) for the league lead in WAR.

For the rest of his career, Davis averaged 3.1 WAR per season, and though he had good years, he never reached 6.0 WAR again. The WAR figure was, again, mostly driven by defense. He had several good offensive seasons, but his only 20-homer season came in 1962 when he was 22, two years prior to his career year.

Davis' career probably looks a lot like PCA's apparent upside before his home run barrage in the first half of 2025. He racked up over 60 career WAR with a decent batting average, excellent defense and excellent baserunning.

With PCA reaching 30-30 last year and 40-40 this year, though, the bar has been raised.

César Cedeño followed up his 8.0 WAR season with a 7.4 season at age 22 and 5.8 at age 23. He was very good through age 29 and, in fact, the most statistically similar player through both ages 27 and 28 was Hall of Famer Carl Yastrzemski. Things fell apart after that. As a quick and easy way to illustrate it, Cedeño amassed 49.4 WAR through his age-29 season and only 3.6 in his final six years.

It's easier to remember that Andruw Jones' career unfolded in similar fashion because we witnessed it not too long ago and then discussed his Hall of Fame candidacy during his nine turns on the ballot before he finally made it in. In Jones' 12 Braves' season, he racked up 368 home runs, 1,117 RBI and 61 WAR while generally being considered the best defensive center fielder in baseball. Once he left, in time for his age-31 season, Jones accrued just 1.7 WAR in his last five seasons.

Through Jones' age-28 season, the most statistically similar players are Bryce Harper and Frank Robinson.

To compare these two to Crow-Armstrong, the argument would be that he'll continue to look like one of the best players in baseball -- even if taking steps backward from being the very best in the 2026 regular season -- before falling apart once he's around 30 years old.

Certainly possible, but given the adjustments he's already not only been willing to make at the plate, but eager to make, I'm just not seeing him all of a sudden hitting a wall. Sure, at some point he'll start losing speed and that'll affect his defensive range and baserunning, but why would the plate skills deteriorate all of a sudden?

Then again, few saw it coming with Jones.

All three of these players were on the path to having an argument as the best player in history before injuries derailed them in their 30s and left them as mere "all-time greats."

Through Mickey Mantle's age-30 season, he had 90.7 WAR. He averaged 121 games per year in his final six seasons, putting up 19.5 WAR. Through  Ken Griffey Jr.'s age-30 season, he had 76.2 WAR. He played 10 more seasons, but only amassed 7.6 more WAR. Through Mike Trout's age-27 season, he had 72.1 WAR (his top statistical similars were Mantle, Robinson and Griffey). We'll skip 2020 for obvious reasons, but since then he's averaged 90 games and 3.0 WAR per season.

Even with the drop-offs, it would be surprising to see Crow-Armstrong reach this level because it's surprising to see any player reach this lofty standard. In career WAR among center fielders, Mantle is fourth, Trout is fifth and Griffey is sixth. They trail only Cobb, Speaker and the guy we're going to discuss next. Joe DiMaggio is seventh.

Despite the lofty standard PCA set for himself in 2026, coming close to Willie Mays' career would be jaw-droppingly shocking, and that is absolutely not intended as a slight.

Mays has an argument as the greatest player in baseball history and he put himself in that discussion by being good at everything and doing it all with great longevity. From his age-23 to -35 seasons, he led the majors in WAR eight times, leading the NL an additional two times. His worst seasons in that timeframe were 7.6 and 7.8 WAR. In that span, he also led the league in runs twice, hits once, triples three times, home runs three times, stolen bases four times, average once, on-base percentage once, slugging percentage five times and OPS five times.

Mays even posted 5.2 WAR at age 39 and 6.3 at age 40. He led the NL with a .425 OBP when he was 40 years old! He hit .345/.411/.667 at age 23.

Mays' 156.2 career WAR ranks third among position players all-time behind two guys you might have heard of: Barry Bonds (162.8) and Babe Ruth (162.3).

I feel pretty strongly Pete Crow-Armstrong isn't headed for a run at this career and that's just fine.

The realistic long-term range for the 2026 National League MVP seems somewhere between Cedeño and Mantle. It's a wide variance of outcomes between those two, obviously, as well it should be when the player we discuss is only 24 years old. For his name to be mentioned among these at all is a testament to the year Crow-Armstrong just put together.

#2812. MLB rumors: Rangers in mix for Sato, Dodgers seek infield help, Korean slugger could join free agent class

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The annual GM Meetings came and went this week with only one significant transaction: Evan Phillips signed with the Braves. Phillips, our 34th-ranked free agent, received a three-year deal worth $35 million. He'll replace Raisel Iglesias in Atlanta's bullpen and pair with Robert Suarez at the end of the game. The Braves could still re-sign Iglesias too, I suppose.

Otherwise, the GM Meetings brought a lot of rumors -- A LOT of rumors -- and presumably the groundwork was laid for moves that could be completed later this offseason. Here are our top 50 free agents and top 25 trade candidates, and the important offseason dates and deadlines you need to know. Here now are Friday's hot stove happenings.

The Rangers are among the teams with interest in Japanese slugger Teruaki Sato, reports The Athletic. Sato primarily played third base for the Hanshin Tigers, though he does not stand out defensively, and could land at first base or in the corner outfield in MLB. Texas could stand to upgrade at first base and could always slot Sato in at DH as well.

We ranked Sato as the fourth-best free agent available and the best free agent position player. The reigning Triple Crown winner in Japan hit .306/.383/.609 with 39 home runs in 2026. Sato, 28 in March, has some similarities to Munetaka Murakami as a lefty with big power and swing-and-miss concerns, though Sato is a much more aggressive hitter and won't walk as much as Murakami.

With Miguel Rojas retired, the Dodgers are looking to add a right-handed hitting infielder, according to The Athletic. Mookie Betts and Max Muncy are entrenched on the left side of the infield. Tommy Edman, a switch-hitter, is penciled in at second base, with fellow switch-hitter Alex Freeland next in line as depth. Freeland has not hit much in his limited MLB time, however.

This is not a great free agent class overall, but it does feature several utility infielders who hit right-handed. Among them are Mauricio Dubón, Ha-Seong Kim, Isiah Kiner-Falefa, and Edmundo Sosa. Glovemen Oswald Peraza (Angels) and Jared Triolo (Pirates) are potential trade targets. Rojas was a very productive role player for Los Angeles. He won't be so easy to replace.

The Padres are receiving calls about catchers Luis Campusano and Freddy Fermin, according to the San Diego Union-Tribune. Top prospect Ethan Salas was called up in September and became the youngest catcher to appear in the postseason in Game 2 of the Wild Card Series. Salas is the future behind the plate, making Campusano or Fermin (or perhaps both) expendable.

Campusano and Fermin are very different players. Campusano is a bat-first catcher who might be worth keeping given his age (28) and the fact that the Padres could slot him at DH. That said, those same qualities make him appealing to other teams. Fermin, 31, is a terrific defender who hasn't hit much the last few years. The Padres need pitching and could deal a catcher or two for an arm or two.

Samsung Lions outfielder Ja-Wook Koo has hired a new agent and is looking to make the jump to MLB, reports MLB.com. Koo, a 10-time All-Star and this year's Korean Series MVP, has been among the best hitters in the Korea Baseball Organization the last few years. The 33-year-old hit .371/.458/.581 with 15 homers and nearly as many walks (66) as strikeouts (71) in 2026.

Koo's age and the fact he's a left-handed hitter in a market inundated with left-handed hitting outfielders will work against him in free agency. He has a great track record in Korea, though, including a history of high contact rates, which could make him a cheaper and more appealing target than MLB free agents like Michael Conforto, Trent Grisham, Cedric Mullins, and Daulton Varsho.

#2811. NASCAR Is Weighing a Pit Stop in Qualifying. Brad Keselowski Says He Pushed for It

Brad Keselowski says he advocated for NASCAR's idea of adding a pit stop to qualifying at a few 2027 points races, breaking with critics Jeff Gluck and Pete Pistone.

John Probst, NASCAR's president of event management, raised the idea Nov. 6 on SiriusXM NASCAR Radio. "We are looking at that All-Star style qualifying where you actually have a pit stop for maybe a very select few races next year," he said.

NASCAR hasn't said how a points-race version would work. At this year's All-Star Race at Dover, the format had each driver run one lap at speed, pit on the second lap for four tires and no fuel, then race back to the start-finish line. The clock ran from the green flag to the checkered flag, so a slow stop cost a driver spots on the grid just like a slow lap.

At Dover, the stop doubled as the $100,000 Pit Crew Challenge. Keselowski won the 2025 All-Star pole at North Wilkesboro under a similar format.

SiriusXM NASCAR Radio host Pete Pistone said the format belongs in the exhibition race and nowhere else.

"If you want to use a pit stop as part of an exhibition race, fine, but in no way, shape or form should that idea migrate to any points race. Qualifying is a necessary procedural function of a race weekend to set the lineup. Trying to make it 'entertaining' has failed before," Pistone wrote on X on Nov. 18.

Longtime NASCAR writer Jeff Gluck agreed, reposting Pistone's comments with a short message: "+1, please don't do this @NASCAR."

Keselowski replied to Gluck on X that evening. "I advocated for and support this move, so I guess we see this differently," he wrote.

The criticism has also come from inside the garage. On the Door Bumper Clear podcast, 23XI Racing spotter Freddie Kraft called the idea a gimmick. He said it belongs in the All-Star Race, not points races.

Probst said NASCAR wants to try the format at a handful of races in the 2027 season and see how fans respond. Talks with teams are ongoing, and NASCAR has not said which races, if any, would use it.

The pit-stop format was one of several qualifying changes Probst discussed. Starting in 2027, owner points alone will set the lineup when qualifying is canceled, replacing the performance metric that NASCAR had signaled it would change. NASCAR is also considering dropping the second round of qualifying at superspeedways.

Keselowski is weighing his own next move following his split with RFK Racing as a driver at the end of 2026. He finished 19th in the standings and has said he has several options, from driving to ownership to TV work.

#2810. Fernando Mendoza’s tests not getting any easier. Broncos will show how much rookie learned

Fernando Mendoza compared what Mike Macdonald did to him Sunday to playing chess.

He left out the part about the chessboard being set up inside an octagon.

The Seahawks' defensive wizard of a head coach challenged the Raiders rookie with shifting coverages and pressure packages, then let his defenders deliver the physical consequences. There were punches, a little taekwondo and enough swift kicks to leave Mendoza studying the board from the canvas.

Fernando Mendoza (15) of the Las Vegas Raiders warms up before a game at Allegiant Stadium in Las Vegas on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Getty Images

Now comes Denver, where another accomplished defensive coach and a rested collection of pass rushers await his second NFL start.

Broncos defensive coordinator Vance Joseph has had the bye week to prepare for the Raiders. Macdonald just provided fresh film of what confused Mendoza, what slowed his decisions and what happened when an encouraging first half gave way to a miserable second.

Joseph will have his own ways of asking whether the rookie learned anything.

That is how this works. Put a weakness on film and the next opponent will make you prove you have addressed it. Fix that one and someone will start looking for another.

Mendoza understood as much after his debut, accepting responsibility for the sacks and acknowledging that Macdonald had fooled him. His willingness to own those mistakes was encouraging. Sunday offers the first opportunity to show what he has done about them.

Brock Bowers #89 and Fernando Mendoza #15 of the Las Vegas Raiders bump fists on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, at Allegiant Stadium in Las Vegas. Getty Images

Mendoza completed 11 of 13 passes for 90 yards in the first half against Seattle, distributing the ball to eight receivers and scoring on a 10-yard quarterback draw. He looked comfortable enough to give the Raiders reason to believe they could win.

Then Seattle adjusted. Mendoza finished the second half 6-of-16 passing, and the Raiders struggled to sustain anything.

The challenge in Denver is recognizing those changes quickly enough to keep the offense moving. That means identifying pressure, trusting the appropriate answer and understanding when a play has reached its expiration date.

It also means getting considerably more help.

The Raiders rushed for 65 yards on 24 attempts against Seattle. Asking a rookie to keep solving difficult passing situations while the running game produces so little is an exhausting way to develop a quarterback.

Klint Kubiak has to find ways to create manageable downs, move Mendoza when the situation calls for it and give him throws he can make decisively. The offensive line must provide enough time for those decisions to matter.

Denver has won five straight games, and Nik Bonitto supplied a reminder of its ability to finish opponents with his late strip-sack against Carolina before the bye.

Mendoza is walking into another difficult classroom, with the crowd adding to the demands.

Someday, he might look back on these first two starts with a debt of gratitude. The lessons could help him recognize trouble sooner, play faster and become the quarterback the Raiders envisioned.

For now, he would probably appreciate learning a few of them without another black eye.

Miller has not been as sharp as the Raiders have come to expect, and this would be a good week to rediscover his best football.

Bonitto remains one of the league's most dangerous rushers, even with his sack production trailing the pace of his recent seasons. His strip-sack against Carolina showed how quickly he can decide a game, and the bye provided time to recharge for the stretch run.

Las Vegas Raiders players celebrate Fernando Mendoza's touchdown against the Seattle Seahawks in Las Vegas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Seattle won 21-13. Getty Images

Miller must handle Bonitto's speed without becoming vulnerable to his counters. Mendoza can help by getting the ball out on time and maintaining his position in the pocket.

But the Raiders need their veteran left tackle to win enough individual battles to give their rookie a chance.

Stokes needs a response game after an uneven season took a conspicuous turn against Seattle.

Kubiak briefly pulled him after plays on which the coach said his effort fell short. The message was clear. A player the Raiders trust to handle difficult coverage assignments still has to finish every down.

Waddle can make any lapse expensive. His speed puts pressure on a cornerback's positioning, and his ability to create separation demands concentration throughout the route.

Stokes must stay disciplined when Bo Nix extends plays and keep competing if the ball goes elsewhere. The Raiders' pass rush also has to finish its work.

Against Denver's most dangerous receiver, coverage and effort will both be under scrutiny.

Mendoza looks more comfortable in his second start, gets the ball out faster and throws his first NFL touchdown pass. There will be enough improvement to give the Raiders something tangible to build on. But their inability to establish a running game keeps putting too much on the rookie, and the defense gives Denver too many opportunities to answer. Coming off the bye, the Broncos have the preparation time and pass rush to force mistakes. Mendoza takes a step forward. The Raiders still come up short.

That many Seahawks defenders pressured Mendoza in his first start, illustrating how much the Raiders must improve their protection before facing Denver's varied rush.

#2808. Could Tarik Skubal take a short-term, high-AAV contract? Breaking down pros and cons

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The expectation is left-hander and two-time AL Cy Young winner Tarik Skubal, far and away the best free agent available this offseason, will sign a record contract before the collective bargaining agreement expires at 11:59 p.m. ET on Dec. 1. Barring a surprise agreement, the owners are almost certain to lock out the players at that time. Skubal is expected to sign before then.

Skubal, 30 later this month, is a Scott Boras client and Boras tends to shatter records, not beat them narrowly. There's a real chance Skubal signs a contract worth more than $400 million in the next two weeks or so. Excluding Shohei Ohtani, who is technically a pitcher but is really a two-way player, here are the largest pitching contracts in history:

Something like, say, eight years at $50 million a year would give Skubal a $400 million contract that obliterates the previous pitcher records for total guarantee and average annual value. Several big-market clubs are said to be pursuing Skubal (Blue Jays, Dodgers, Mets, etc.), who is a special free agent as an in-his-prime ace. Conditions are ripe for a record deal.

There is also a chance Skubal goes the Valdez route, or the Bo Bichette/Kyle Tucker route, and signs a short-term contract with a high annual salary. It might only be a small chance, but there is a chance. The Athletic reports the Dodgers prefer such an arrangement with Skubal. ESPN says Skubal has no interest in such a deal, though minds can always be changed.

With that in mind, here are the pros and cons of a high-dollar, short-term contract for Skubal. Keep in mind that such a contract is usually a three- or four-year deal with opt outs, not a straight one- or two-year contract, and Skubal wouldn't necessarily have to use the opt outs to re-enter free agency. He could leverage them into a new contract to stay with his team.

1. Maximize earnings. Plain and simple, a short-term contract would give Skubal a chance to max out his earnings by making free agency available to him again as soon as next offseason. Go out, have a typical Skubal season in 2027, and perhaps market forces push the demand even higher than it is right now. Remember that, along with a salary cap, there could be a salary floor in the future. Small-market contenders like, say, the Guardians or Rays could throw many millions at Skubal to reach the floor in the future.

2. Chance to set more records. This ties back to the last point. Skubal already set a new salary record for an arbitration-eligible player ($32 million in 2026) and he's positioned to set a new standard for pitcher contracts this offseason. Go back into free agency in a year or two, and Skubal (and Boras) could have a chance to break his own pitcher contract record. The record itself won't be a driving factor in that decision (the money will ultimately decide things), but it would be a nice feather in his cap.

3. Freedom to change teams. Sometimes things don't work out. I don't think Juan Soto expected to miss the postseason in the first two years of his record 15-year, $765 million contract with the Mets. It's entirely possible Skubal could find his next situation not to his liking for whatever reason. Maybe the team doesn't contend, maybe he clashes with the coaching staff, maybe he doesn't like the local breakfast taco options. There could be a million different reasons it doesn't work. A short-term, high-dollar deal would give Skubal an escape hatch and allow him to head elsewhere if things go sideways.

1. Injury and performance risk. All things considered, Skubal is a pretty safe bet on a long-term contract, though he is a pitcher, and pitchers get hurt. It's what they do. Skubal has had three elbow surgeries, including Tommy John surgery in 2017 and a minor procedure this May. If he gets hurt again, or his performance slips for whatever reason, then he could wind up costing himself in the big picture because he won't be able to opt out and sign a larger deal. A short-term, high-dollar contract will pay him handsomely. He won't struggle to put bread on the table. But injury/performance risk means Skubal could ultimately leave money on the table.

2. Potential salary cap. It is possible the next CBA will include a salary cap, which could limit Skubal's market and earning potential should he go back out into free agency in a year or two (or three). It's not just the cap itself either. MLB has proposed contract limits as part of the salary cap: five years at 15% of the cap for players who change teams, or six years at 16% of the cap for players who remain with their current team. Even if MLB doesn't get a salary cap and instead the competitive balance tax system remains in place with stiffer penalties, that could cost Skubal in the future. This offseason might be his last chance to sign in a truly free market.

Capitals legend Alex Ovechkin, the NHL's all-time goals king, said Monday that this would be the final season for the 41-year-old. The news didn't come as a surprise, but the timing might have been a little unusual. In any case, the decision adds a lot of significance to Wednesday's home opener against Pittsburgh. The Russian said he decided this summer that the 2026-27 campaign would be his last, but that he purposefully waited until ahead of the home opener: "It was important for me to make this announcement before our first home game, so I have the chance to thank all the people around the game, everyone who has been so important to me and my family and helped me through the good times and tough losses," Ovechkin wrote. "I wouldn't have been able to achieve everything I have in my life without them. I am excited about this season with my teammates, and we will do everything we can to make this year special."

In July, Ovechkin signed a one-year deal with the Caps worth $4.25 million – he gets a whopping $4.75 million performance bonus when he plays in his 10th game -- after playing in all 82 last year and putting up yet another 30-goal season with 32. It was his NHL-record 20th 30-goal season. But his average ice time dipped to a career-low 17:27. Through two games this year, Ovechkin is averaging only 13:10 of ice time and has an assist.

Ovi ranks No. 1 in league history in the regular season in goals (929), power-play goals (331), game-winning goals (141), empty-net goals (72) and overtime goals (27). But if we include playoffs, Ovechkin's 1,006 goals are second behind Wayne Gretzky's 1,016. You know Ovechkin wants that mark too and barring injury should get it. If he scores 20 goals this season, he'll tie Gordie Howe for the most 20-goal campaigns at 22.

He's +225 to get one tonight. Ovechkin has scored on 189 different goalies during his NHL career but not yet against Pittsburgh's 25-year-old Arturs Silovs (2-0-1, 2.46 GAA). Silovs was a backup in Vancouver his first three NHL seasons and only got regular time last season with Pittsburgh, so Ovechkin has only faced him twice. Silovs allowed eight goals in two starts last year vs. Washington, splitting them. Ovi did score in one of them, but on an empty net.

Silovs started both of Pittsburgh's first two wins this year, but backup Sergei Murashov was in net for Tuesday's 3-2 home loss to Winnipeg. Crosby was held without a point. He has 1,109 career assists, two shy of tying Ray Bourque (1,111 with Boston) for the most with a single franchise in NHL history. Crosby needs one helper to move past Joe Thornton (1,109) for seventh place on the league's all-time list and is +105 for an assist.

The NHL is not what it is today without Ovechkin and Crosby, and they entered the league together in 2005. They have faced off 100 times in the NHL, including 25 games in the playoffs. In those 100, they have combined for 231 points. A total of 90 games saw either find the score sheet and 49 had both record a point in the same game.

They have met in four postseason series, each in the second round. Crosby and the Penguins won the first three series and won the Cup each time. The Capitals won in 2018 and won their first and still only Stanley Cup. They have played 75 regular-season games in the series. Crosby leads 43-28-4 and has 35 goals and 62 assists in those. Ovechkin has 38 goals and 33 helpers.

The 39-year-old Crosby is in the final year of his contract but has said he has no plans to retire. He and Ovechkin have joined Howe, Alex Delvecchio, Stan Mikita and Steve Yzerman as the only players in NHL history to play 22 seasons with one franchise.

"Over the years, obviously, I've developed a friendship with him and have a ton of respect for him," Crosby said to the media Monday with the Ovechkin news. "It's tough, you come into the league at 18 years old, and before we even met, we were built up against each other."

It should be Logan Thompson in net for Washington, and he was excellent in his season debut in allowing two goals in a win at defending Stanley Cup champion Carolina last Friday. Last season at home, he was 19-9-5 with a 2.18 GAA and three shutouts. Thompson made two starts vs. Pittsburgh overall last campaign and won both with a 1.50 GAA.

The Penguins and Capitals have three meetings left, including Washington's home finale on April 4. That should be quite the Sunday night if both Crosby and Ovechkin are healthy then. Tickets on the secondary market are north of $800 to get in.

#2806. Blue Jays' Anthony Santander issues statement after sharing anti-Muslim videos: 'This content caused hurt'

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Toronto Blue Jays outfielder Anthony Santander has issued a statement regarding his recent anti-Muslim social media posts that he has since deleted. Santander's statement, which he posted to Instagram and follows a public condemnation of the posts by Blue Jays general manager Ross Atkins, reads as follows:

"After taking time to reflect on my recent social media posts and speaking with people I trust and respect, I recognize that I should have been more thoughtful about what I shared on my platform.

"I realize that this content caused hurt and I sincerely apologize. I should have been more thoughtful about what I shared, recognizing the responsibility that comes with having a public voice.

"As a man of faith, it is my belief that every person deserves to be treated with dignity and respect, regardless of their religion, background, or beliefs. After hearing feedback from those I hurt, I realize that the content I shared fell short of my own convictions.

"I would like to affirm my respect for the Muslim community and for the dignity and freedom of people of every faith and background.

"From this experience, I have learned to ensure that none of my communications promote hate or give the impression that I support hatred, discrimination, or violence against anyone."

The club had earlier released a statement on the incident that read:

"Baseball is for everyone, and the Blue Jays have created an inclusive experience where all fans feel welcome at our ballpark. Individual comments do not represent our organization's values and beliefs."

Santander, a Venezuela native, shared social media videos following the recent U.S. midterm elections, including one in which a Christian pastor said, "There is no such thing as kind Muslims." Another suggested that Sharia law would be imposed in the U.S. following the election of Abdul El-Sayed to the Senate representing the state of Michigan.

Atkins told reporters Tuesday that Santander's behavior "did not align" with organizational values and there were no words "to express my level of disappointment."

"I talked to Anthony about his lack of awareness, realizing that he has a very large microphone, what that meant," Atkins said. "We're working exceptionally hard internally to ensure, and we have been, that it's a safe environment, that baseball is for everyone. It's very important to us. It's a part of our organizational values and his behavior did not align with that."

Santander, 32, joined the Blue Jays prior to the 2025 season on a five-year, $92.5 million free-agent contract. He struggled badly across 54 games in 2025 and missed the entire 2026 season with injury.

#2803. 49ers had enough to worry about — then Vikings got Kirk Cousins

The 49ers were already going to learn something about themselves Sunday night in Mexico City.

Then the Vikings went and got Kirk Cousins.

Minnesota Vikings quarterback Kirk Cousins (8) looks on during the fourth quarter against the Green Bay Packers in Green Bay, Wis., Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

What had the makings of a classic confrontation between San Francisco's offense and Minnesota's defense suddenly became something considerably more revealing. The Vikings have a quarterback their head coach trusts, throwing to receivers he knows, with a defense capable of carrying a team deep into January.

For the 49ers, that makes this a timely examination of where they stand among the NFC's best.

They will arrive in Mexico City with an offense averaging a league-leading 399.8 yards and 31.2 points per game, second in the NFL. Minnesota counters by allowing a league-low 14 points per game and 273.3 yards, second fewest.

The deeper numbers reinforce the matchup. The 49ers lead the league in offensive points per possession at three. The Vikings allow a league-low 1.24 points per possession.

Kyle Shanahan has the personnel and imagination to make even a good defense look uncomfortable. Brock Purdy can distribute the ball to multiple threats, and Christian McCaffrey's ability to move between the backfield and the passing game forces opponents to account for him wherever he lines up.

Brian Flores has spent the season making quarterbacks uncomfortable before they even take the snap. His fronts invite questions about who is coming, who is dropping and how much time the quarterback will have to sort through it.

Watching those two work against each other would have justified the trip by itself.

But Cousins' return changes the demands on San Francisco's defense, too.

Minnesota acquired him from the Raiders last week and immediately put him back in an offense he operated under Kevin O'Connell in 2022 and 2023. Five days after the trade, Cousins threw for 207 yards and two touchdowns in a 30-10 win at Green Bay.

Brock Purdy (13) of the San Francisco 49ers looks to pass against the Dallas Cowboys on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, at AT&T Stadium in Arlington, Texas. Getty Images

There was an interception mixed in. There will be adjustments as he catches up with everything that has changed since he left.

Still, the Vikings suddenly look capable of making an opponent pay for devoting its entire week to figuring out their defense.

Cousins understands O'Connell's expectations. He knows how Justin Jefferson sees coverage and where Jordan Addison can hurt it. Those relationships give Minnesota a chance to accelerate a transition that normally requires considerably more time.

And they give the 49ers a second problem to solve.

San Francisco just scored 46 points against Dallas and needed nearly every one of them. Allowing 40 points in a victory leaves plenty to address, especially with O'Connell getting another week to prepare his reunited offense.

The 49ers can welcome that challenge. Their 8-1 record has earned them a place near the front of the conference. Beating a 7-2 Minnesota team with this defense and a familiar quarterback settling back in would provide another reason to believe they can stay there.

San Francisco 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan, left, and general manager John Lynch watch players warm up on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, in Arlington, Texas. AP Photo/Julio Cortez

Sunday offers the kind of opponent that can expose a weakness while there is still time to fix it.

Or confirm that the 49ers have fewer of them than almost everyone else.

Two coaches operating at the top of their games will spend Sunday trying to take away what makes the other so effective.

Shanahan wants Purdy playing with clarity, using motion, formations and the threat of the run to create favorable throws. Flores wants that picture changing, preferably after Purdy has committed to what he thinks he sees.

McCaffrey could determine who gets his way. Sending him into a route gives Purdy another dangerous option. Keeping him in protection helps account for the pressure Flores brings.

Shanahan's challenge is preserving enough receiving options to punish Minnesota's aggression. For Flores, it is forcing quick decisions without making the answers obvious.

Every protection adjustment and late defensive movement will have a purpose.

O'Connell and Morris helped the Rams win a Super Bowl following the 2021 season, with O'Connell coordinating the offense and Morris running the defense. Those practice field confrontations now become part of the preparation for a game with considerable NFC implications.

O'Connell has his quarterback back, and with Cousins comes the freedom to call plays knowing how the man executing them will respond. That familiarity makes Minnesota feel more complete.

Minnesota Vikings head coach Kevin O'Connell looks on after a game against the Green Bay Packers at Lambeau Field in Green Bay, Wis., Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Morris understands how O'Connell builds an offense and how its concepts fit together. He can use that knowledge to challenge Cousins' first read and force him to hold the ball.

O'Connell will anticipate those adjustments, of course.

That is what makes this compelling. Two former colleagues know how much the other knows, and both must decide when to break their tendencies.

Something feels right about Cousins finding his way back to Minnesota after 2 ½ seasons away, with his recovery from the Achilles injury that ended his 2023 season part of that journey. He returns to a more complete team, with a defense capable of giving him a chance to finish what he started. The 49ers will make Minnesota earn every bit of this. But with another week alongside O'Connell, Cousins makes enough timely throws, and Flores finds one late stop to give the reunion another memorable chapter.

The Vikings sacked Jordan Love eight times in Cousins' return last Sunday. Purdy's protection will face a considerably tougher test than the reunion storyline alone suggests.

#2802. Rangers lose star goaltender Igor Shesterkin for season with lower-body injury; what's next for New York?

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

New York Rangers goaltender Igor Shesterkin will miss the remainder of the season with a lower-body injury, the team announced on Saturday.

Shesterkin suffered an apparent leg injury in the Rangers' loss to the Washington Capitals on Friday night as his right leg slid awkwardly into the post while scrambling to get back in position. Shesterkin fell to the ice immediately, and he needed assistance to get down the tunnel.

Prior to that loss, the Rangers were off to a terrific 4-1-0 start, and Shesterkin was a driving force behind that success. In just five appearances, Shesterkin posted 3.90 goals saved above average with a .900 high-danger save percentage, according to Natural Stat Trick.

The 2022 Vezina Trophy winner, Shesterkin has put up sparkling numbers since he entered the NHL in 2019-20. In 330 games, Shesterkin boasts a .917 save percentage, a 2.51 GAA and 22 shutouts. Over the last three full seasons from 2023-26, Shesterkin ranked third in goals saved above average with 45.4. Only Connor Hellebuyck and Andrei Vasilevskiy were better in that category.

Shesterkin was a big reason why the Rangers felt they could bounce back from a disappointing 2025-26 season, and losing the star goaltender puts a huge dent in the team's playoff hopes. It doesn't help that backup goalie Joonas Korpisalo is also hurt.

Dylan Garand replaced Shesterkin on Friday night, and Spencer Martin will be recalled from the AHL after recovering from his own injury. With the Rangers playing well early, it wouldn't be a shock to see them explore better replacement options for Shesterkin.

New York won't be able to replace Shesterkin's impact because he's one of the five best goalies in the world. However, the team should be able to upgrade its current goaltending situation, which is a fairly low bar to clear.

First, there are unsigned veterans still on the open market in Petr Mrazek and James Reimer. Neither one was great last season -- hence their availability at the moment -- Reimer and Mrazek could provide a level of stability in the crease that the Rangers simply don't have right now.

New York could also explore trade possibilities, and Minnesota Wild netminder Filip Gustavsson could be a solution there. Jesper Wallstedt is the future of the crease in Minnesota, making Gustavsson more of a luxury. Would the Wild send Gustavsson to New York in exchange for some draft capital it could use to acquire a center?

The one snag in a potential Gustavsson deal is his contract. Gustavsson is in the first year of a deal that pays him $6.8 million over the next five seasons. That's a big commitment for a player who will become a backup when Shesterkin returns.

Obviously, Connor Hellebuyck has been on the trade market for months now, but that trade wouldn't make sense for the Rangers. Hellebuyck is on an expensive long-term deal, and New York would have $20 million tied up in goalies if they made that move, not to mention the astronomical acquisition cost.

However, if you'll allow me to get creative here, it's no secret that the Florida Panthers have pursed Hellebuyck. What if the Panthers traded Jacob Markstrom -- just acquired in the offseason -- to the Rangers in order to make room for Hellebuyck?

Markstrom does have a 20-team no-trade list, so the question is whether New York would be one of his 11 approved teams. Florida flipping Markstrom so quickly seems like a long shot, but the team has taken plenty of big swings in recent history.

#2801. MLB rumors: Blue Jays targeting Ketel Marte, Giants rejecting trade calls for ace Logan Webb

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The annual GM Meetings came and went this week with two significant transactions. The Braves signed reliever Evan Phillips (three years and $35 million) and the Red Sox signed slugger Jake Bauers (four years and $58 million). Bauers will give Boston some sorely needed home run power. Phillips will pair with Robert Suarez in the late innings for Atlanta.

Otherwise, the GM Meetings brought a lot of rumors and presumably the groundwork was laid for moves that could be completed later this offseason. Here are our top 50 free agents and top 25 trade candidates, and the important offseason dates and deadlines you need to know. Here now are Saturday's hot stove happenings.

The Blue Jays have interest in Diamondbacks second baseman Ketel Marte, who has a full no-trade protection, reports Sportsnet. It does not appear a trade is imminent. We ranked Marte among the offseason's top trade candidates despite his odd AWOL incidents the last few seasons. Even at 33, he remains one of the game's best second basemen.

The Blue Jays went 79-83 this past season largely because of their offense, which ranked dead last in runs and home runs. They also ranked 17th in WAR at second base as All-Star Ernie Clement ran out of steam in the second half. Offensively, Marte is coming off his worst 162-game season since 2018, though he would still represent a clear upgrade for the Blue Jays.

The Giants continue to decline trade calls about ace righty Logan Webb, reports USA Today. Webb, 30 next week, had his worst full season in the big leagues in 2026, in part due to a nagging knee injury, though he is signed affordably through 2028. San Francisco needs to add to its rotation, not subtract one of the game's best and most durable starters.

That said, a Webb trade would presumably net the Giants multiple young players and improve the team's talent base. It never hurts to listen, but for now, the Giants seem disinclined to move their best pitcher since Madison Bumgarner was in his prime. Webb and Landen Roupp are the only locks for San Francisco's 2027 rotation at the moment.

Not surprisingly, the Braves want to add at least one starting pitcher, according to The Athletic. They could also re-sign Raisel Iglesias or bring in another late-inning reliever even after signing Phillips. Chris Sale and Grant Holmes are Atlanta's only returning healthy starters. Bryce Elder (knee), Spencer Schwellenbach (elbow) and Spencer Strider (elbow) all finished the season injured.

The Braves will give Didier Fuentes, who was so dominant as a reliever this past season, a chance to start in 2027. It will be difficult to count on him for a full season, however. Fuentes, 21, threw a career-high 97 innings between the minors, majors, and postseason in 2026. Atlanta will need to monitor his workload next year. Another starter to soak up innings is a must this offseason.

The Tigers have interest in free agent left-hander Shota Imanaga, reports MLB.com. Jackson Jobe, Troy Melton, River Ryan, and Framber Valdez are presumably locks for Detroit's rotation. Ty Madden and Keider Montero are depth options. Reese Olson is expected back from shoulder surgery early next year. Still, the Tigers could clearly use another starting pitcher.

We ranked Imanaga, 33, as the 30th-best free agent available this offseason. He has been very home run prone the last two years (1.9 homers per nine innings), which could make him a good match for what is now called Fifth Third Park. Detroit's stadium tends to play pitcher-friendly, particularly in April and September (and October), when the ball doesn't carry well in the cold.

The Blue Jays are receiving calls about infielder Ernie Clement and utility man Davis Schneider, reports The Athletic. Teams have also inquired about catcher Brandon Valenzuela, who was a Gold Glove finalist as a rookie in 2026. Toronto reportedly has interest in Jazz Chisholm Jr. as it seeks more of a power bat on the infield, potentially making Clement expendable.

Schneider, 27, had a poor 2026 and spent time in Triple-A, though he is versatile and has hit lefties in spots over the years. There is a shocking shortage of quality right-handed bats throughout baseball right now. Schneider's heavy plate discipline approach and ability to play left field as well as some infield positions make him an interesting enough target this offseason.

The Cubs, Padres, and White Sox are among several teams pursuing Japanese right-hander Kaima Taira, reports the San Diego Union-Tribune. Taira, 26, had a 1.36 ERA in 145 innings for the Seibu Lions in 2026. We ranked him as the 19th-best free agent available this offseason, though he could ultimately wind up in the bullpen given his height (5-foot-8).

The Padres and two Chicago teams need starting pitching this offseason (they need multiple starters, really) and would likely use Taira in their rotation, at least to begin the season. For what it's worth, Taira told Sanspo he does not have a preferred destination, geographically. The Cubs, Padres, and White Sox would all give him a chance to pitch for a contender right away.

The Cardinals have declined trade inquiries into first baseman Alec Burleson and shortstop Masyn Winn, according to the St. Louis Post-Dispatch. St. Louis is also receiving interest in catchers Jimmy Crooks and Pedro Pagés, who are more likely to be traded. Burleson and Winn are off-limits, though, and considered part of the long-term future in St. Louis.

Leo Bernal, who was so impressive during his September call up, and top prospect Rainiel Rodriguez are the future behind the plate for the Cardinals. Pagés is a traditional glove-first backup catcher. Crooks has some offensive upside thanks to his hard-hit ability (exit velocity, etc.), and he rates well as a pitch-framer and thrower. He's an appealing target for catcher-needy teams.

#2800. Pistons vs. Raptors final score: Cade vs. Scottie battle goes to Raps

Two former teammates and two Eastern Conference teams traded body blows all night. A Cade Cunningham 3-pointer to put the Pistons up 102-101 with 20 seconds left was answered by a Scottie Barnes driving layup over two defenders to give the Raptors the lead they would not relinquish. When a Kevin Huerter three came up short as the buzzer sounded, it was decided. Raptors 105-102.

Cunningham led Detroit with 23 points and nine assists, but he was outshined on this night by his former Montverde teammate Barnes, who led all scorers with 29 points and hit his first nine shots of the night.

While it was appointment television watching the two top players on each team matched up with each other, the rest of the game was pretty much a horror show for the Pistons. Distilling why the Pistons dropped this game will sound very familiar to Pistons fans — not owning the paint.

Detroit surrendered 54 points inside to Toronto and gave up crucial offensive rebounds late. It's one thing to see Barnes put a great game on you, but Detroit was owned inside by rookie Allen Graves making his first start of the season. He scored 20 points, had 10 rebounds, including four on the offensive end. Detroit simply had no answer for him. Certainly not John Collins.

The overmatched emergency starting center struggled again in just under 20 minutes of action. He was just 1-of-4 from the floor for three points and six rebounds. The return of Isaiah Joe from injury isn't looking very different from when he was playing early in the season — he's still struggling to find his shot. Joe was 1-of-6 from deep tonight. Getting those kinds of performances from your two highest-profile offseason acquisitions is not the recipe for winning basketball.

The other major issue tonight is that you were facing one of the worst-shooting offenses in the NBA and on this night, they could not miss. Some of that had to do with Detroit's occasionally passive defense. But tonight, the Raptors players were just collectively on one. We already covered how hot Barnes was, especially early, but the sweet shooting was up and down the roster.

The rookie Graves was 6-of-9, Jakob Poeltl was 5-of-7, and Immanuel Quickly was 6-of-10. Collectively, Toronto shot 67% inside the arc tonight. Again, I'm going to point to the big black hole in the middle of Detroit's lineup. A combination of John Collins and Paul Reed as your only big men is just not going to get it done in the NBA. The Pistons need Jalen Duren to come back desperately.

The loss to the Raptors drops the Pistons to 1-1 in NBA Cup play, but then again, I refuse to care about the NBA Cup. Detroit now goes on a mini road trip to Atlanta on Sunday and Orlando on Tuesday before enjoying seven of the next eight games at home.
